can you tell me again, what's the reason for this message? (the session ids are not from my machine but from a colleague)

Operation stopped by hook: Another session is active: "let's start theming then and also put the       
  others in the i..."                                                                                    
                                                                                                         
  You can continue here, but checkpoints from both sessions will be interleaved.

> AGENT

This warning appears when starting a new Claude Code session while **another session already has uncommitted checkpoints** in the same repository.

**What's happening:**

The manual-commit strategy stores checkpoints on a shadow branch (`entire/<HEAD-hash>`). When your colleague started a new session, the CLI detected that:

1. A previous session ("let's start theming then...") already exists
2. That session has checkpoints that haven't been condensed yet (no user commit since then)
3. Both sessions would write to the same shadow branch

**Why it matters:**

If they continue, checkpoints from both sessions will be **interleaved** on the same shadow branch. This works fine, but:
- When listing rewind points, they'll see checkpoints from both sessions mixed together
- Each checkpoint shows its `SessionID` and `SessionPrompt` to help distinguish them
- On the next commit, all sessions get condensed together

**Their options:**

1. **Continue anyway** - Both sessions coexist, checkpoints interleave
2. **Resume the other session** - `claude -r e2f39195-8421-468f-a161-0e8a86ec3767` picks up where the previous session left off
3. **Suppress the warning** - `entire enable --disable-multisession-warning` if this is expected workflow

**Common causes:**
- Forgot to commit after a previous Claude session
- Multiple terminal windows/worktrees in the same repo
- Teammate left a session running

## Analysis Report: Auto-Commit Strategy's Empty Checkpoint Prevention

Based on my review of `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go`, here's my detailed analysis:

### 1. Does it check for actual code changes before creating a commit/checkpoint?

**YES - It checks at multiple levels:**

#### Level 1: Early Check in commitCodeToActive() (lines 200-209)
```go
// Check if there are any code changes to commit
if len(ctx.ModifiedFiles) == 0 && len(ctx.NewFiles) == 0 && len(ctx.DeletedFiles) == 0 {
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "No code changes to commit to active branch\n")
    // Return current HEAD hash but mark as not created
    head, err := repo.Head()
    if err != nil {
        return commitCodeResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
    }
    return commitCodeResult{CommitHash: head.Hash(), Created: false}, nil
}
```

This is the **first validation** - if the SaveContext has no file changes, it immediately returns with `Created: false` without attempting any git operations.

#### Level 2: Late Check via HEAD Comparison (lines 239-244)
```go
// Check if a new commit was actually created by comparing with HEAD before
created := commitHash != headBefore.Hash()
if created {
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Committed code changes to active branch (%s)\n", commitHash.String()[:7])
}
return commitCodeResult{CommitHash: commitHash, Created: created}, nil
```

Even if files were staged, this compares the resulting commit hash against the HEAD before attempting the commit. This catches the edge case where files were modified but already committed.

#### Level 3: The commitOrHead() Helper Function (lines 40-54)
```go
func commitOrHead(repo *git.Repository, worktree *git.Worktree, msg string, author *object.Signature) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
    commitHash, err := worktree.Commit(msg, &git.CommitOptions{Author: author})
    if errors.Is(err, git.ErrEmptyCommit) {
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "No changes to commit (files already committed)\n")
        head, err := repo.Head()
        if err != nil {
            return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
        }
        return head.Hash(), nil  // Returns HEAD, not a new commit
    }
    if err != nil {
        return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to commit: %w", err)
    }
    return commitHash, nil
}
```

This handles the go-git `ErrEmptyCommit` case where the commit operation would result in no changes.

---

### 2. What happens if SaveChanges is called but no files were modified?

**Empty checkpoints are PREVENTED:**

The control flow shows a three-layer defense:

```
SaveChanges()
├─ commitCodeToActive()
│  ├─ Level 1: Check if ctx has files → return Created=false (lines 201-208)
│  └─ Level 2: Compare HEAD before/after → return Created=false if no new commit
│
└─ If Created=false:
   └─ Skip metadata creation entirely (lines 159-167)
```

**Specific code showing the prevention (lines 157-167):**
```go
// If no code commit was created (no changes), skip metadata creation
// This prevents orphaned metadata commits that don't correspond to any code commit
if !codeResult.Created {
    logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
    logging.Info(logCtx, "checkpoint skipped (no changes)",
        slog.String("strategy", "auto-commit"),
        slog.String("checkpoint_type", "session"),
    )
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last commit)\n")
    return nil
}

// Step 2: Commit metadata to entire/sessions branch using sharded path
// Only reached if codeResult.Created is true
_, err = s.commitMetadataToMetadataBranch(repo, ctx, cpID)
```

---

### 3. Summary Table

| Scenario | Result | Code Reference |
|----------|--------|-----------------|
| No files in SaveContext | Created=false early return | Lines 201-208 |
| Files staged but already committed | Created=false (HEAD unchanged) | Lines 239-244 |
| git.ErrEmptyCommit from go-git | Created=false (commitOrHead returns HEAD) | Lines 42-48 |
| Any of above conditions | **Metadata creation skipped** | Lines 159-167 |
| Code commit actually created | **Both code AND metadata committed** | Lines 169-174 |

---

### Key Design Points

1. **Orphaned Metadata Prevention**: The comment on line 158 explicitly states the intent: *"This prevents orphaned metadata commits that don't correspond to any code commit"*

2. **Dual Validation**: Uses both context-level checks (SaveContext) and git-level checks (HEAD comparison) to catch edge cases

3. **User Feedback**: Clear stderr messages for each scenario:
   - "No code changes to commit to active branch" (line 202)
   - "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last commit)" (line 165)
   - "No changes to commit (files already committed)" (line 43)

4. **Result Structure**: The `commitCodeResult` struct with a `Created` bool field (lines 191-194) makes intent clear and enables the gate-keeping logic

**Conclusion**: The auto-commit strategy **actively prevents empty checkpoints** through multiple validation layers. No orphaned metadata commits can be created without a corresponding code commit.

## Analysis Report: SaveChanges() Implementation in Manual-Commit Strategy

Based on my review of the code, here are the findings about how SaveChanges() handles empty or no-change checkpoints:

### 1. **YES - SaveChanges() DOES check for file changes before creating checkpoints**

The deduplication mechanism is implemented at the `checkpoint.WriteTemporary()` level and is **automatically checked** by SaveChanges(). Here's the flow:

**In `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` (lines 23-158):**

```go
// SaveChanges saves a checkpoint to the shadow branch.
// Uses checkpoint.GitStore.WriteTemporary for git operations.
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext) error {
    // ... initialization ...
    
    // Use WriteTemporary to create the checkpoint (lines 83-98)
    result, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteTemporaryOptions{
        SessionID:         sessionID,
        BaseCommit:        state.BaseCommit,
        ModifiedFiles:     ctx.ModifiedFiles,
        NewFiles:          ctx.NewFiles,
        DeletedFiles:      ctx.DeletedFiles,
        MetadataDir:       ctx.MetadataDir,
        MetadataDirAbs:    ctx.MetadataDirAbs,
        CommitMessage:     ctx.CommitMessage,
        AuthorName:        ctx.AuthorName,
        AuthorEmail:       ctx.AuthorEmail,
        IsFirstCheckpoint: isFirstCheckpointOfSession,
    })
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to write temporary checkpoint: %w", err)
    }

    // If checkpoint was skipped due to deduplication (no changes), return early (lines 100-111)
    if result.Skipped {
        logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
        logging.Info(logCtx, "checkpoint skipped (no changes)",
            slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
            slog.String("checkpoint_type", "session"),
            slog.Int("checkpoint_count", state.CheckpointCount),
            slog.String("shadow_branch", shadowBranchName),
        )
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last checkpoint)\n")
        return nil
    }
    // ... rest of checkpoint handling ...
}
```

### 2. **HOW the deduplication works - Tree Hash Comparison**

In `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/temporary.go` (lines 46-125):

```go
// WriteTemporary writes a temporary checkpoint to a shadow branch.
// If the new tree hash matches the last checkpoint's tree hash, the checkpoint
// is skipped to avoid duplicate commits (deduplication).
func (s *GitStore) WriteTemporary(ctx context.Context, opts WriteTemporaryOptions) (WriteTemporaryResult, error) {
    // ... setup ...
    
    // Get the last checkpoint's tree hash for deduplication (lines 68-74)
    var lastTreeHash plumbing.Hash
    if parentHash != plumbing.ZeroHash {
        if lastCommit, err := s.repo.CommitObject(parentHash); err == nil {
            lastTreeHash = lastCommit.TreeHash
        }
    }

    // Collect all files to include (lines 76-90)
    var allFiles []string
    if opts.IsFirstCheckpoint {
        // For the first checkpoint of this session, capture ALL files in working directory
        allFiles, err = collectWorkingDirectoryFiles()
        if err != nil {
            return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to collect working directory files: %w", err)
        }
    } else {
        // For subsequent checkpoints, only include modified/new files
        allFiles = make([]string, 0, len(opts.ModifiedFiles)+len(opts.NewFiles))
        allFiles = append(allFiles, opts.ModifiedFiles...)
        allFiles = append(allFiles, opts.NewFiles...)
    }

    // Build tree with changes (line 93)
    treeHash, err := s.buildTreeWithChanges(baseTreeHash, allFiles, opts.DeletedFiles, opts.MetadataDir, opts.MetadataDirAbs)

    // Deduplication: skip if tree hash matches the last checkpoint (lines 98-104)
    if lastTreeHash != plumbing.ZeroHash && treeHash == lastTreeHash {
        return WriteTemporaryResult{
            CommitHash: parentHash,
            Skipped:    true,
        }, nil
    }

    // Create checkpoint commit with trailers (lines 106-124)
    commitMsg := trailers.FormatShadowCommit(opts.CommitMessage, opts.MetadataDir, opts.SessionID)
    commitHash, err := s.createCommit(treeHash, parentHash, commitMsg, opts.AuthorName, opts.AuthorEmail)
    // ... error handling and branch update ...
}
```

### 3. **What Gets Compared - The Entire Tree**

The comparison is **not** just about file lists, but about the **actual git tree hash**:
- All modified/new files are collected
- A new git tree is built from the base tree with these changes
- The tree hash of this new tree is compared to the previous checkpoint's tree hash
- If they match → **skipped** (no actual changes)
- If different → checkpoint is created

### 4. **Test Verification**

In `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/checkpoint_test.go` (lines 350-472), there's explicit test coverage:

```go
// TestWriteTemporary_Deduplication verifies that WriteTemporary skips creating
// a new commit when the tree hash matches the previous checkpoint.
func TestWriteTemporary_Deduplication(t *testing.T) {
    // ... setup ...
    
    // First checkpoint should be created
    result1, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), WriteTemporaryOptions{
        SessionID:         "test-session",
        BaseCommit:        baseCommit,
        ModifiedFiles:     []string{"test.go"},
        MetadataDir:       ".entire/metadata/test-session",
        IsFirstCheckpoint: true,
    })
    if !result1.Skipped { // First checkpoint NOT skipped
        t.Error("first checkpoint should not be skipped")
    }

    // Second checkpoint with identical content should be skipped
    result2, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), WriteTemporaryOptions{
        // ... same modified files ...
    })
    if !result2.Skipped { // Second checkpoint IS SKIPPED
        t.Error("second checkpoint with identical content should be skipped")
    }

    // After modifying the file...
    result3, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), WriteTemporaryOptions{
        // ... same modified files, but content changed ...
    })
    if result3.Skipped { // Third checkpoint NOT skipped
        t.Error("third checkpoint with modified content should NOT be skipped")
    }
}
```

### 5. **User Feedback When Skipped**

When a checkpoint is skipped due to no changes:

**To stderr (line 109 of manual_commit_git.go):**
```
Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last checkpoint)
```

**To logs (.entire/logs/):**
```json
{
  "strategy": "manual-commit",
  "checkpoint_type": "session",
  "checkpoint_count": N,
  "shadow_branch": "entire/<hash>",
  "message": "checkpoint skipped (no changes)"
}
```

### Summary

| Aspect | Implementation |
|--------|-----------------|
| **Empty checkpoints prevented?** | ✅ YES - via tree hash deduplication |
| **Change comparison method** | Git tree hash equality (structural + content) |
| **Compared against** | Previous checkpoint on same shadow branch |
| **User feedback** | "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last checkpoint)" |
| **Return value** | `WriteTemporaryResult.Skipped = true`, no error |
| **Session state updated?** | NO - skipped checkpoints don't increment `CheckpointCount` |
| **Test coverage** | `TestWriteTemporary_Deduplication` in checkpoint_test.go |

The implementation is **robust** - it prevents creating duplicate checkpoints by comparing the actual git tree structure rather than just file lists, ensuring no wasted checkpoint history.

## Report: WHEN and HOW SaveChanges() is Called

Based on my analysis of the codebase, here's a comprehensive breakdown of when checkpoint saves are triggered and what conditions are checked:

### **CALLERS OF SaveChanges()**

SaveChanges() is called from TWO hook handler files:

1. **`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:629`**
   - Function: `commitWithMetadata()` 
   - Hook: Claude Code's "Stop" hook (fires when user exits Claude or session ends)
   - Conditions checked BEFORE calling SaveChanges():
     - Line 535-544: Checks if ANY code changes exist: `totalChanges := len(relModifiedFiles) + len(relNewFiles) + len(relDeletedFiles)`
     - If `totalChanges == 0`, **SKIPS checkpoint creation entirely** and returns early (no SaveChanges called)
     - Line 580: Ensures strategy setup is complete

2. **`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hooks_geminicli_handlers.go:412`**
   - Function: `commitGeminiSession()` (called by both `commitWithMetadataGemini()` and `handleGeminiAfterAgent()`)
   - Hook: Gemini CLI's "AfterAgent" hook (fires after each user prompt/response cycle)
   - Conditions checked BEFORE calling SaveChanges():
     - Line 353-361: Same check as Claude Code: `totalChanges := len(relModifiedFiles) + len(relNewFiles) + len(relDeletedFiles)`
     - If `totalChanges == 0`, **SKIPS checkpoint creation entirely** and returns early

### **INTERNAL CHECKS WITHIN SaveChanges()**

Both strategies have ADDITIONAL deduplication checks:

#### **Manual-Commit Strategy** (`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go:23`)
- Calls `store.WriteTemporary()` which implements deduplication via tree hash comparison
- **Logic** (in `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/temporary.go:99-103`):
  - Computes tree hash of current changes
  - Compares to last checkpoint's tree hash
  - If trees match: **SKIPS checkpoint** and returns `Skipped: true`
  - If trees differ OR no previous checkpoint: **Creates checkpoint**
- **Code** (lines 99-111 in manual_commit_git.go):
  ```go
  if result.Skipped {
      logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
      logging.Info(logCtx, "checkpoint skipped (no changes)",
          slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
          ...
      )
      fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last checkpoint)\n")
      return nil
  }
  ```

#### **Auto-Commit Strategy** (`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go:137`)
- Calls `commitCodeToActive()` which checks if code changes exist
- **Logic** (lines 200-244 in auto_commit.go):
  ```go
  // Line 201: Check for empty changes
  if len(ctx.ModifiedFiles) == 0 && len(ctx.NewFiles) == 0 && len(ctx.DeletedFiles) == 0 {
      fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "No code changes to commit to active branch\n")
      head, err := repo.Head()
      return commitCodeResult{CommitHash: head.Hash(), Created: false}, nil
  }
  ```
  - Line 240: Detects if actual commit was created: `created := commitHash != headBefore.Hash()`
  - Line 159-167: If no commit created, **SKIPS metadata** and returns early:
    ```go
    if !codeResult.Created {
        logging.Info(logCtx, "checkpoint skipped (no changes)",...)
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last commit)\n")
        return nil
    }
    ```

### **FLOW SUMMARY**

```
Hook fires (Stop/AfterAgent)
    ↓
Check: totalChanges == 0?
├─ YES → Skip SaveChanges() entirely, return early (lines 536-543 in Claude, 354-360 in Gemini)
└─ NO → Call SaveChanges()
    ↓
Manual-Commit Strategy:
    ├─ WriteTemporary() compares tree hash with last checkpoint
    ├─ Tree hash matches? → Skip checkpoint (no file changes)
    └─ Tree hash differs? → Create checkpoint
    
Auto-Commit Strategy:
    ├─ commitCodeToActive() checks len(ModifiedFiles/NewFiles/DeletedFiles)
    ├─ All empty after staging? → Created: false, skip metadata
    ├─ No new commit created? → Skip checkpoint early (lines 159-167)
    └─ Commit created? → Write metadata to entire/sessions branch
```

### **KEY FILES AND LINE NUMBERS**

| File | Lines | Purpose |
|------|-------|---------|
| hooks_claudecode_handlers.go | 534-544 | Pre-SaveChanges check for no changes |
| hooks_claudecode_handlers.go | 629-631 | Call to SaveChanges() |
| hooks_geminicli_handlers.go | 353-361 | Pre-SaveChanges check for no changes |
| hooks_geminicli_handlers.go | 412-414 | Call to SaveChanges() |
| checkpoint/temporary.go | 98-104 | Manual-commit deduplication logic |
| manual_commit_git.go | 100-111 | Manual-commit skip handling |
| auto_commit.go | 200-209 | Auto-commit pre-stage check |
| auto_commit.go | 159-167 | Auto-commit skip if no commit created |

### **CRITICAL INSIGHT**

There are **TWO LAYERS** of "has code changed" checks:

1. **Hook Layer** (before SaveChanges is called): Counts modified/new/deleted files from transcript parsing
2. **Strategy Layer** (inside SaveChanges): Manual-commit uses tree hash deduplication, Auto-commit checks if actual git commit was created

This means a checkpoint can be skipped at EITHER level, preventing unnecessary checkpoints when no actual code changes occurred.

## Summary: Empty Checkpoint Prevention

Based on the review, **checkpoints will NOT be created if there are no code changes**. There are multiple layers of protection:

### Layer 1: Hook Level (before SaveChanges is called)

In `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:534-544`:
```go
totalChanges := len(relModifiedFiles) + len(relNewFiles) + len(relDeletedFiles)
if totalChanges == 0 {
    // Returns early - SaveChanges() is NEVER called
}
```

### Layer 2: Strategy Level (inside SaveChanges)

**Manual-commit** (`checkpoint/temporary.go:98-104`):
- Computes git tree hash of current changes
- Compares to last checkpoint's tree hash
- If trees match → returns `Skipped: true`, no checkpoint created

**Auto-commit** (`auto_commit.go:200-209`):
- Checks if `len(ModifiedFiles) + len(NewFiles) + len(DeletedFiles) == 0`
- Also compares HEAD before/after to catch edge cases
- If no new commit → skips metadata creation

### User Feedback

Both strategies print to stderr when skipping:
```
Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last checkpoint)
```

### Conclusion

The system is designed correctly - **empty checkpoints are prevented** at multiple levels. If your colleague is seeing checkpoints created without code changes, that would be a bug. The most likely scenarios where this could happen:

1. **Metadata-only changes** - but these are explicitly not checkpointed (hook layer checks code files only)
2. **Bug in file detection** - the transcript parsing might incorrectly identify files as changed
3. **First checkpoint edge case** - the first checkpoint captures all working directory files, which might behave differently

## Report: HEAD Change Validation in Claude Code Hook Handlers

Based on my analysis of the codebase, here's what I found regarding HEAD change detection and validation during active sessions:

### **Key Finding: No Explicit HEAD Change Validation in Stop Hook**

The "Stop" hook (`commitWithMetadata()` in `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`) does **NOT** validate whether HEAD has changed since the session started. It performs these operations:

1. **Loads session state** (line 444) - which contains `BaseCommit` (the HEAD when session started)
2. **Creates metadata and transcript files** 
3. **Calls `strategy.SaveChanges()`** to persist the checkpoint
4. **Does NOT verify** that current HEAD matches `sessionState.BaseCommit`

### **No "Wrong Branch" or "Base Commit Mismatch" Error Handling**

The Stop hook has:
- No check comparing `currentHead` vs `sessionState.BaseCommit`
- No error returned if they differ
- No warning about potential rebase/pull/commit operations that changed HEAD
- No path that rejects the checkpoint due to HEAD mismatch

### **HEAD Change Detection Exists (But Not in Stop Hook)**

However, HEAD change detection **does exist elsewhere**:

**1. In `checkConcurrentSessions()` (line 148-155):**
```go
head, err := repo.Head()
if err != nil {
    // Output user-friendly error message
    ...
}
```
Gets current HEAD but doesn't validate against `BaseCommit`.

**2. In `PrepareCommitMsg()` hook (line 252-282):**
```go
// Get current HEAD to compare with session base commits
head, err := repo.Head()
if err != nil {
    return nil
}
currentHeadHash := head.Hash().String()

// Filter to sessions where BaseCommit matches current HEAD
for _, session := range sessions {
    if session.BaseCommit == currentHeadHash {
        currentSessions = append(currentSessions, session)
    }
}
```
This **does validate** that `BaseCommit == currentHeadHash` when deciding whether to reuse checkpoint IDs.

**3. Shadow Branch Migration (line 876-908 in `manual_commit_hooks.go`):**
The post-commit hook **detects** when HEAD changed:
```go
// Check if HEAD has moved (user pulled/rebased or committed)
if state.BaseCommit != "" && state.BaseCommit != currentHead {
    // This happens when user does: stash → pull → stash apply, or rebase, etc.
    // Move shadow branch to new base commit
    oldBaseCommit := state.BaseCommit
    newBaseCommit := head.Hash().String()
    state.BaseCommit = newBaseCommit
```
This **automatically migrates** the shadow branch rather than rejecting it.

### **Git Sequence Operations Detection**

The code **does detect** when git is in a rebase/cherry-pick/revert state:

In `PrepareCommitMsg()` hook (line 141-173):
```go
func isGitSequenceOperation() bool {
    // Check for rebase state directories
    if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "rebase-merge")); err == nil {
        return true
    }
    if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "rebase-apply")); err == nil {
        return true
    }
    // Check for cherry-pick and revert state files
    if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "CHERRY_PICK_HEAD")); err == nil {
        return true
    }
    if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "REVERT_HEAD")); err == nil {
        return true
    }
}
```

If true, the prepare-commit-msg hook **skips adding a checkpoint trailer** (line 193-198).

### **Missing Validation Scenario**

What is **NOT checked** in the Stop hook:

1. **No HEAD validation before saving checkpoint** - If user does `git checkout`, `git reset`, or `git rebase` between session start and Stop, the Stop hook doesn't detect this
2. **No validation that current working directory matches session's base commit**
3. **No warning or error** if HEAD diverged from the session's starting point
4. **No fallback behavior** - it proceeds with SaveChanges regardless

### **Behavior if HEAD Changes**

If a rebase occurs mid-session:

1. **During rebase** (in sequence operation): 
   - `prepare-commit-msg` skips adding checkpoint trailer (won't create new commits)
   
2. **After rebase completes**:
   - `PostCommit` hook detects `state.BaseCommit != head.Hash()` and automatically migrates the shadow branch to new HEAD (lines 876-908)
   - Session continues without error

3. **During Stop hook** (the checkpoint creation):
   - No validation occurs
   - Session state is saved with whatever current HEAD is
   - If HEAD changed since session start, the checkpoint is created relative to new HEAD
   - Shadow branch migration (if needed) happens in PostCommit, not Stop

### **Summary of Checks**

| Check | Exists? | Location | Behavior |
|-------|---------|----------|----------|
| HEAD matches BaseCommit on Stop hook | NO | - | No validation |
| HEAD matches BaseCommit on PrepareCommitMsg | YES | manual_commit_hooks.go:267 | Filters which sessions to reuse |
| Git sequence operation detection | YES | manual_commit_hooks.go:149-173 | Skips trailer if rebasing |
| HEAD change detection | YES | manual_commit_hooks.go:876 | Auto-migrates shadow branch |
| Error on base commit mismatch | NO | - | None |
| Warning on base commit mismatch | NO | - | None |

The design appears intentionally permissive: it **detects and handles HEAD changes automatically** rather than rejecting them, allowing sessions to span rebase/pull operations seamlessly.

## Investigation Report: Session State Base Commit Tracking in Manual-Commit Strategy

### 1. Where SessionState is Stored and Fields

**Storage Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go`

**Stored in:** `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json` (using git's common directory, shared across worktrees)

**Key Fields in SessionState:**
```go
type State struct {
    SessionID string              // Unique session identifier
    BaseCommit string             // HEAD commit when the session started (CRITICAL)
    WorktreePath string           // Absolute path to the worktree root
    StartedAt time.Time           // When the session was started
    CheckpointCount int           // Number of checkpoints created
    CondensedTranscriptLines int  // Lines already included in previous condensation
    UntrackedFilesAtStart []string// Files that existed at session start
    FilesTouched []string         // Files modified/created/deleted during session
    ConcurrentWarningShown bool   // True if user was warned about concurrent sessions
    LastCheckpointID id.CheckpointID // Checkpoint ID from last condensation
    AgentType agent.AgentType     // Human-readable agent name (e.g., "Claude Code")
    TokenUsage *agent.TokenUsage  // Accumulated token usage
    // ... plus attribution tracking and transcript position fields
}
```

The `BaseCommit` field is the **commit hash of HEAD when the session started** and is the critical tracking field for the manual-commit strategy.

---

### 2. When BaseCommit is Set and Updated

**INITIAL SET - During Session Initialization:**

Called from `InitializeSession()` (line 784 in `manual_commit_hooks.go`):

```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) initializeSession(repo *git.Repository, 
    sessionID string, agentType agent.AgentType, transcriptPath string) (*SessionState, error) {
    
    head, err := repo.Head()  // Get current HEAD
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
    }
    
    state := &SessionState{
        SessionID:    sessionID,
        BaseCommit:   head.Hash().String(),  // SET HERE - captures HEAD at session start
        WorktreePath: worktreePath,
        StartedAt:    time.Now(),
        CheckpointCount: 0,
        // ... rest of fields
    }
    
    if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
        return nil, err
    }
    return state, nil
}
```

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` (lines 195-238)

**UPDATED - When HEAD Changes (Pull/Rebase Detection):**

Detected in `InitializeSession()` hook handler (lines 876-909 in `manual_commit_hooks.go`):

```go
// Check if HEAD has moved (user pulled/rebased or committed)
if state.BaseCommit != head.Hash().String() {
    oldBaseCommit := state.BaseCommit
    newBaseCommit := head.Hash().String()
    
    // Check if old shadow branch exists - if so, user did NOT commit 
    // (would have been deleted on commit). This happens when user does: 
    // stash → pull → stash apply, or rebase, etc.
    oldShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(oldBaseCommit)
    oldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)
    
    if oldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true); err == nil {
        // Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit
        newShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(newBaseCommit)
        newRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)
        
        // Create new reference pointing to same commit
        newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())
        if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {
            return fmt.Errorf("failed to create new shadow branch: %w", newShadowBranch, err)
        }
        
        // Delete old reference
        if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {
            fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch: %v\n", oldShadowBranch)
        }
        
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Moved shadow branch from %s to %s (base commit changed)\n",
            oldShadowBranch, newShadowBranch)
    }
    
    state.BaseCommit = newBaseCommit  // UPDATE HERE
    needSave = true
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Updated session base commit to %s\n", newBaseCommit[:7])
}
```

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` (lines 876-909)

### 3. What Happens When SaveChanges is Called - Uses Stored BaseCommit

**SaveChanges Does NOT Re-Read HEAD** - it uses the stored `BaseCommit` from session state:

```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext) error {
    repo, err := OpenRepository()
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
    }
    
    // Extract session ID from metadata dir
    sessionID := filepath.Base(ctx.MetadataDir)
    
    // Load session state (including stored BaseCommit)
    state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to load session state: %w", err)
    }
    
    // Initialize if state is nil OR BaseCommit is empty
    if state == nil || state.BaseCommit == "" {
        // Preserve existing AgentType if we have a partial state
        agentType := DefaultAgentType
        if state != nil && state.AgentType != "" {
            agentType = state.AgentType
        }
        state, err = s.initializeSession(repo, sessionID, agentType, "")
        if err != nil {
            return fmt.Errorf("failed to initialize session: %w", err)
        }
    }
    
    // Use stored BaseCommit to create shadow branch name
    shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)
    branchExisted := store.ShadowBranchExists(state.BaseCommit)
    
    // Pass state.BaseCommit (NOT current HEAD) to WriteTemporary
    result, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteTemporaryOptions{
        SessionID:      sessionID,
        BaseCommit:     state.BaseCommit,  // Use STORED value, not current HEAD
        ModifiedFiles:  ctx.ModifiedFiles,
        NewFiles:       ctx.NewFiles,
        DeletedFiles:   ctx.DeletedFiles,
        // ... rest of options
    })
    
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to write temporary checkpoint: %w", err)
    }
    
    // Update state after checkpoint
    state.CheckpointCount++
    // ... rest of state updates
    
    // Save updated state
    if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to save session state: %w", err)
    }
}
```

**Key Points:**
- Lines 33-48: Loads stored `state.BaseCommit` from session state file
- Line 57: Uses `state.BaseCommit` to derive shadow branch name (NOT current HEAD)
- Line 85: Passes `state.BaseCommit` to `WriteTemporary()` for checkpoint creation
- **Never re-reads HEAD** to determine base commit for checkpoint creation

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` (lines 23-158)

### 4. HEAD Change Detection Logic

**WHERE HEAD Changes Are Detected:** `InitializeSession()` hook handler in `manual_commit_hooks.go` (lines 876-909)

**DETECTION MECHANISM:**

```go
if state.BaseCommit != head.Hash().String() {
    // HEAD has moved since session started
    oldBaseCommit := state.BaseCommit
    newBaseCommit := head.Hash().String()
```

**WHAT THIS DETECTS:**
- User performed `git pull`, `git rebase`, `git checkout`, or other operations that change HEAD
- **WITHOUT creating a commit** (because if they committed, the shadow branch would have been deleted)

**SHADOW BRANCH MIGRATION (Automatic):**

When HEAD changes are detected and old shadow branch still exists:

1. **Check if old shadow branch exists** - if it does, user didn't commit (a commit would delete it)
2. **Rename/move the shadow branch** from `entire/<old-commit-hash>` to `entire/<new-commit-hash>`
3. **Update state.BaseCommit** to the new HEAD
4. **User sees message:** "Moved shadow branch from entire/abc1234 to entire/def5678 (base commit changed after pull/rebase)"

**Detection Code (lines 885-907):**
```go
oldShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(oldBaseCommit)
oldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)

if oldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true); err == nil {
    // Old shadow branch exists - create new branch pointing to same commit
    newShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(newBaseCommit)
    newRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)
    
    newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())
    if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to create new shadow branch: %w", newShadowBranch, err)
    }
    
    // Delete old reference
    if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch: %v\n", oldShadowBranch)
    }
    
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Moved shadow branch from %s to %s\n", oldShadowBranch, newShadowBranch)
}

state.BaseCommit = newBaseCommit  // UPDATE THE STORED BASE COMMIT
```

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` (lines 876-909)

## Shadow Branch Migration Logic Investigation Report

Based on my analysis of the manual-commit strategy codebase, here's the complete picture of shadow branch migration:

### 1. WHERE IS THE BASE COMMIT STORED AND CHECKED?

**Storage Location:** `.git/entire-sessions/` directory
- Each active session has a state file: `<session-id>.json`
- File location is managed by `session.StateStore` (in `cmd/entire/cli/session/`)
- The `BaseCommit` field is in the `SessionState` struct at line 21 of `manual_commit_types.go`:

```go
type SessionState struct {
	SessionID                string          `json:"session_id"`
	BaseCommit               string          `json:"base_commit"`  // ← Stored here
	WorktreePath             string          `json:"worktree_path,omitempty"`
	// ... more fields ...
}
```

**Initial Check:** Session initialization in `initializeSession()` (lines 195-238 of `manual_commit_session.go`)
```go
state := &SessionState{
	SessionID:          sessionID,
	BaseCommit:         head.Hash().String(),  // ← Set to current HEAD at session start
	// ...
}
```

### 2. WHAT TRIGGERS SHADOW BRANCH MIGRATION/RENAMING?

**Migration is triggered in `InitializeSession()` hook (lines 876-909 of `manual_commit_hooks.go`):**

The migration logic is:
1. Check if HEAD has moved: `state.BaseCommit != head.Hash().String()` (line 877)
2. If HEAD changed, check if old shadow branch exists (line 885)
3. If it exists: Move branch by:
   - Creating new ref at new hash: `entire/<NEW-HEAD-HASH[:7]>`
   - Deleting old ref: `entire/<OLD-BASE-COMMIT[:7]>`
   - Updating session state with new BaseCommit

**Code excerpt (lines 876-909):**
```go
// Check if HEAD has moved (user pulled/rebased or committed)
if state.BaseCommit != head.Hash().String() {
    oldBaseCommit := state.BaseCommit
    newBaseCommit := head.Hash().String()

    // Check if old shadow branch exists - if so, user did NOT commit (would have been deleted)
    // This happens when user does: stash → pull → stash apply, or rebase, etc.
    oldShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(oldBaseCommit)
    oldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)
    if oldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true); err == nil {
        // Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit
        newShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(newBaseCommit)
        newRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)

        // Create new reference pointing to same commit
        newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())
        if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {
            return fmt.Errorf("failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w", newShadowBranch, err)
        }

        // Delete old reference
        if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {
            // Non-fatal: log but continue
            fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\n", oldShadowBranch, err)
        }

        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Moved shadow branch from %s to %s (base commit changed after pull/rebase)\n",
            oldShadowBranch, newShadowBranch)
    }

    state.BaseCommit = newBaseCommit
    needSave = true
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Updated session base commit to %s\n", newBaseCommit[:7])
}
```

### 3. WHEN IS THIS CHECK DONE? (SESSION START ONLY OR BEFORE EACH SaveChanges?)

**CRITICAL FINDING: The check is done ONLY AT SESSION START, not before SaveChanges**

The migration logic lives in `InitializeSession()` which is called:
- **Once per prompt** (when user submits a new prompt)
- During session initialization via git hook

**NOT called before SaveChanges()** (line 23 of `manual_commit_git.go`):
```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext) error {
	repo, err := OpenRepository()
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
	}

	// Extract session ID from metadata dir
	sessionID := filepath.Base(ctx.MetadataDir)

	// Load or initialize session state
	state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
	// ... NO HEAD check here ...
```

### 4. WHAT HAPPENS IF HEAD CHANGES MID-SESSION (Between Prompts)?

**Scenario:** User session with checkpoint on commit A → User runs `git rebase` → More checkpoints try to save

**Safe Case (Migration Works):**
1. User submits new prompt → `InitializeSession()` called
2. HEAD has changed: A → B
3. Old shadow branch `entire/A` exists
4. Migration triggered:
   - Create `entire/B` pointing to same commit as `entire/A`
   - Delete `entire/A`
   - Update `state.BaseCommit` to B
5. Next checkpoint saves to correct shadow branch (`entire/B`)

**Unsafe Case (Race Condition #1 - No Migration Detection):**
1. Session initialized at HEAD=A, checkpoints saved to `entire/A`
2. User runs `git rebase` (HEAD → B) **WITHOUT submitting a new prompt**
3. Claude continues running, calls `SaveChanges()`
4. SaveChanges loads state, sees `BaseCommit=A`
5. Gets shadow branch name: `entire/A[:7]` (line 57 of `manual_commit_git.go`)
6. **Tries to write to `entire/A` but HEAD is now at B**
7. Checkpoint saves to wrong shadow branch!

**Code evidence (SaveChanges, lines 32-48):**
```go
// Load or initialize session state
state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
if err != nil {
    return fmt.Errorf("failed to load session state: %w", err)
}
// Initialize if state is nil OR BaseCommit is empty
if state == nil || state.BaseCommit == "" {
    // ... initialize ...
}
// No check for HEAD change here - goes straight to checkpoint creation
```

Then at line 57:
```go
shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)
// ^ Uses OLD BaseCommit, assumes HEAD hasn't changed
```

### 5. POTENTIAL RACE CONDITIONS

#### **Race Condition #1: Mid-Session Rebase Without Prompt**
- **When:** User rebases while Claude is working (between prompts)
- **Impact:** Checkpoint saves to wrong shadow branch
- **Evidence:** `SaveChanges()` doesn't check HEAD vs `state.BaseCommit`
- **Severity:** HIGH - data written to incorrect branch

#### **Race Condition #2: Concurrent Shadow Branch Operations**
- **When:** CreateNew reference succeeds but RemoveReference fails (line 897)
- **Result:** Both `entire/A` and `entire/B` exist pointing to same commit
- **Impact:** Next session initialization may pick wrong branch
- **Code:** Line 897-900 marks old delete as "non-fatal"
```go
if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {
    // Non-fatal: log but continue
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\n", ...)
}
```

#### **Race Condition #3: Session State Save Timing**
- **When:** Shadow branch moves but state save fails (line 912)
- **Result:** Session state still has old BaseCommit, but shadow branch is at new location
- **Impact:** Next SaveChanges uses old BaseCommit, writes to wrong branch
- **Code:** Lines 906-914
```go
state.BaseCommit = newBaseCommit
needSave = true  // ← Set flag but...
// ... later ...
if needSave {
    if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("failed to update session state: %w", err)
    }
}
// No rollback of SetReference if state save fails
```

#### **Race Condition #4: Atomic Check-Act Window**
- **When:** Between checking HEAD (line 877) and migrating (lines 885-904)
- **Scenario:** Multiple concurrent sessions or git operations
- **Impact:** Head changes again, migration creates wrong reference
- **No Lock:** Session state file is not locked during migration

### 6. SAVE CHANGES CODE PATH ANALYSIS

**Current flow (manual_commit_git.go SaveChanges):**
```
LoadSessionState(sessionID)
    ↓
Use state.BaseCommit to get shadow branch name
    ↓
Write checkpoint to shadow branch
    ↓
Update state.CheckpointCount
    ↓
SaveSessionState()
```

**Missing:** No HEAD check before using `state.BaseCommit`

### 7. KEY FILES INVOLVED

| File | Purpose | Line Range |
|------|---------|-----------|
| `manual_commit_session.go` | Load/save session state | 18-120 |
| `manual_commit_hooks.go` | InitializeSession with migration logic | 784-967 |
| `manual_commit_git.go` | SaveChanges checkpoint creation | 23-158 |
| `manual_commit_types.go` | SessionState struct definition | 1-47 |
| `manual_commit_reset.go` | Shadow branch deletion | 17-95 |

### 8. SPECIFIC MIGRATION CODE LINES

- **Base commit comparison:** Line 877 of `manual_commit_hooks.go`
- **Old shadow branch existence check:** Line 885
- **Reference creation:** Lines 891-894
- **Reference deletion:** Lines 897-900
- **State update:** Lines 906-907
- **State persistence:** Lines 911-914

### Summary

The shadow branch migration logic is **fragile** because:

1. **Migration only happens on prompt submission**, not before each SaveChanges
2. **No atomic transaction** for branch rename + state update
3. **Non-fatal delete** can leave duplicate branches
4. **No check in SaveChanges** that HEAD matches state.BaseCommit
5. **Session state can become stale** if migration fails after branch rename

The safest scenario is when the user commits (which triggers condensation and cleans up the shadow branch), or when they submit a new prompt (which triggers migration). The **most dangerous scenario** is a rebase/pull **mid-session without a new prompt**, where SaveChanges will write to an orphaned shadow branch.

## Yes, there's a potential issue!

The agents found a **race condition** in the shadow branch migration logic:

### The Problem

**Shadow branch migration only happens when a new prompt is submitted** (in `InitializeSession`), NOT before each `SaveChanges()`.

Here's the dangerous sequence:

```
1. Session starts at commit A
   → Shadow branch: entire/a1b2c3d
   → state.BaseCommit = A

2. Checkpoints save to entire/a1b2c3d ✓

3. User does rebase (HEAD: A → B)
   → No hook fires yet
   → state.BaseCommit still = A

4. Claude continues working, SaveChanges() called
   → Loads state.BaseCommit = A (stale!)
   → Derives shadow branch: entire/a1b2c3d
   → Writes checkpoint to WRONG branch (orphaned from new HEAD B)

5. User submits new prompt
   → InitializeSession detects HEAD changed
   → Migration triggers (too late!)
   → Renames entire/a1b2c3d → entire/b2c3d4e
```

### The Bug

In `manual_commit_git.go:57`, SaveChanges uses the **stored** `state.BaseCommit`:

```go
shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)
```

It never checks if current HEAD still matches `state.BaseCommit`.

### Impact

If a rebase happens **mid-prompt** (while Claude is working):
- Checkpoints are written to a shadow branch that's now orphaned from HEAD
- The shadow branch name no longer corresponds to the current commit
- Migration only fixes this on the **next** prompt submission

### What's Missing

A HEAD validation check in `SaveChanges()`:

```go
// This check doesn't exist:
currentHead, _ := repo.Head()
if currentHead.Hash().String() != state.BaseCommit {
    // Trigger migration or error
}
```

### Workaround

Don't rebase while Claude is actively working. Wait for a prompt to complete, then rebase, then submit a new prompt (which triggers migration).

Would you like me to draft a fix for this?

> DEVELOPER

He is pretty sure claude did rebase. So we can't really prevent that. would this still fit? So basically a tool call would cause a rebase

> AGENT

Ah, that's a critical detail - **Claude itself doing the rebase** via a tool call!

This is worse because:

### The Scenario

```
1. Session starts at commit A
   → state.BaseCommit = A
   → Shadow branch: entire/a1b2c3d

2. User prompt: "rebase my branch onto main"

3. Claude runs: git rebase main
   → HEAD changes: A → B (new rebased commit)
   → state.BaseCommit still = A (never updated mid-prompt)

4. Claude continues working, maybe edits more files

5. Prompt ends, Stop hook fires, SaveChanges() called
   → Loads state.BaseCommit = A (stale!)
   → Writes checkpoint to entire/a1b2c3d
   → But HEAD is now B!
```

### Why Migration Doesn't Help

Migration only triggers on **new prompt submission** (`InitializeSession`). But:
- The rebase happened **within** the same prompt
- No new prompt = no migration check
- The Stop hook doesn't validate HEAD vs BaseCommit

### The Result

- Checkpoint saved to `entire/a1b2c3d` (based on old commit A)
- But commit A might not even exist anymore after rebase!
- The shadow branch is now orphaned/meaningless
- Rewind points would reference commits that don't exist in the rebased history

### Does This Explain the Concurrent Session Warning?

Not directly - that warning is about session **state files**, not shadow branches. But it could create related confusion:

- If the colleague started a new session after the rebase
- New session sees HEAD = B, different from old session's BaseCommit = A
- System might detect the old session as "active" with uncommitted checkpoints
- But those checkpoints are on a shadow branch that's now orphaned

### The Fix Needed

SaveChanges should detect HEAD changes and either:
1. **Fail fast** with an error ("HEAD changed during session, cannot save checkpoint")
2. **Auto-migrate** before saving (move shadow branch to new HEAD)
3. **At minimum**: warn the user that checkpoint may be orphaned

Want me to look at implementing a fix?

> DEVELOPER

given that we can't fail in the middle of claude doing this, I would like to look at   2. Auto-migrate before saving (move shadow branch to new HEAD)

> AGENT

Let me look at the existing migration code and SaveChanges to understand how to implement this.

   154→		slog.Bool("branch_created", !branchExisted),
   155→	)

   286→// mergeFilesTouched merges multiple file lists into existing touched files, deduplicating.
   287→func mergeFilesTouched(existing []string, fileLists ...[]string) []string {
   288→	seen := make(map[string]bool)
   289→	for _, f := range existing {
   290→		seen[f] = true
   291→	}
   292→
   293→	for _, list := range fileLists {
   294→		for _, f := range list {
   295→			seen[f] = true
   296→		}
   297→	}
   298→
   299→	result := make([]string, 0, len(seen))
   300→	for f := range seen {
   301→		result = append(result, f)
   302→	}
   303→
   304→	// Sort for deterministic output
   305→	sort.Strings(result)
   306→	return result
   307→}
   308→
   309→// accumulateTokenUsage adds new token usage to existing accumulated usage.
   310→// If existing is nil, returns a copy of incoming. If incoming is nil, returns existing unchanged.
   311→func accumulateTokenUsage(existing, incoming *agent.TokenUsage) *agent.TokenUsage {
   312→	if incoming == nil {
   313→		return existing
   314→	}
   315→	if existing == nil {
   316→		// Return a copy to avoid sharing the pointer
   317→		return &agent.TokenUsage{
   318→			InputTokens:         incoming.InputTokens,
   319→			CacheCreationTokens: incoming.CacheCreationTokens,
   320→			CacheReadTokens:     incoming.CacheReadTokens,
   321→			OutputTokens:        incoming.OutputTokens,
   322→			APICallCount:        incoming.APICallCount,
   323→			SubagentTokens:      incoming.SubagentTokens,
   324→		}
   325→	}
   326→
   327→	// Accumulate values
   328→	existing.InputTokens += incoming.InputTokens
   329→	existing.CacheCreationTokens += incoming.CacheCreationTokens
   330→	existing.CacheReadTokens += incoming.CacheReadTokens
   331→	existing.OutputTokens += incoming.OutputTokens
   332→	existing.APICallCount += incoming.APICallCount
   333→
   334→	// Accumulate subagent tokens if present
   335→	if incoming.SubagentTokens != nil {
   336→		existing.SubagentTokens = accumulateTokenUsage(existing.SubagentTokens, incoming.SubagentTokens)
   337→	}
   338→
   339→	return existing
   340→}
   341→
   342→// deleteShadowBranch deletes a shadow branch by name using an existing repo handle.
   343→// Returns nil if the branch doesn't exist (idempotent).
   344→func deleteShadowBranch(repo *git.Repository, branchName string) error {
   345→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName)
   346→
   347→	// Check if reference exists
   348→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   349→	if err != nil {
   350→		// Branch doesn't exist - nothing to delete (idempotent)
   351→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Not an error condition - branch already gone
   352→	}
   353→
   354→	// Delete the reference
   355→	if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(ref.Name()); err != nil {
   356→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to delete branch %s: %w", branchName, err)
   357→	}
   358→
   359→	return nil
   360→}

## Implementation Plan

### 1. Extract migration logic into a reusable helper

```go
// migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded checks if HEAD has changed since session started
// and migrates the shadow branch to the new base commit if needed.
// Returns true if migration occurred.
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {
    head, err := repo.Head()
    if err != nil {
        return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
    }
    
    currentHead := head.Hash().String()
    if state.BaseCommit == currentHead {
        return false, nil // No migration needed
    }
    
    // HEAD changed - migrate shadow branch
    oldShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)
    newShadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(currentHead)
    
    oldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)
    if oldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true); err == nil {
        // Old shadow branch exists - move it
        newRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)
        newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())
        if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {
            return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w", newShadowBranch, err)
        }
        
        if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {
            fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\n", oldShadowBranch, err)
        }
        
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Moved shadow branch from %s to %s (HEAD changed during session)\n",
            oldShadowBranch, newShadowBranch)
    }
    
    state.BaseCommit = currentHead
    return true, nil
}
```

// Check if HEAD has changed (e.g., Claude did a rebase) and migrate if needed

}

    if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {

}
