    28→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    29→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.
    30→var testBinaryPath string
    31→
    32→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    33→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    34→func getTestBinary() string {
    35→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    36→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    37→	}
    38→	return testBinaryPath
    39→}
    40→
    41→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for integration tests.
    42→type TestEnv struct {
    43→	T                *testing.T
    44→	RepoDir          string
    45→	ClaudeProjectDir string
    46→	GeminiProjectDir string
    47→	SessionCounter   int
    48→}
    49→
    50→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated test environment.
    51→// It creates temp directories for the git repo and agent project files.
    52→// Note: Does NOT change working directory to allow parallel test execution.
    53→// Note: Does NOT use t.Setenv to allow parallel test execution - CLI commands
    54→// receive the env var via cmd.Env instead.
    55→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    56→	t.Helper()
    57→
    58→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    59→	// This ensures the CLI subprocess and test use consistent paths
    60→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    61→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    62→		repoDir = resolved
    63→	}
    64→	claudeProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    65→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(claudeProjectDir); err == nil {
    66→		claudeProjectDir = resolved
    67→	}
    68→	geminiProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    69→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(geminiProjectDir); err == nil {
    70→		geminiProjectDir = resolved
    71→	}
    72→
    73→	env := &TestEnv{
    74→		T:                t,
    75→		RepoDir:          repoDir,
    76→		ClaudeProjectDir: claudeProjectDir,
    77→		GeminiProjectDir: geminiProjectDir,
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	// Note: Don't use t.Setenv here - it's incompatible with t.Parallel()
    81→	// CLI commands receive ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR or ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR via cmd.Env instead
    82→
    83→	return env
    84→}
    85→
    86→// Cleanup is a no-op retained for backwards compatibility.
    87→//
    88→// Previously this method restored the working directory after NewTestEnv changed it.
    89→// With the refactor to remove os.Chdir from NewTestEnv:
    90→// - Temp directories are now cleaned up automatically by t.TempDir()
    91→// - Working directory is never changed, so no restoration is needed
    92→//
    93→// This method is kept to avoid breaking existing tests that call defer env.Cleanup().
    94→// New tests should not call this method as it serves no purpose.
    95→//
    96→// Deprecated: This method is a no-op and will be removed in a future version.
    97→func (env *TestEnv) Cleanup() {
    98→	// No-op - temp dirs are cleaned up by t.TempDir()
    99→}
   100→
   101→// cliEnv returns the environment variables for CLI execution.
   102→// Includes both Claude and Gemini project dirs so tests work for any agent.
   103→func (env *TestEnv) cliEnv() []string {
   104→	return append(os.Environ(),
   105→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+env.ClaudeProjectDir,
   106→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+env.GeminiProjectDir,
   107→	)
   108→}

   111→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLI(args ...string) string {
   112→	env.T.Helper()
   113→	output, err := env.RunCLIWithError(args...)
   114→	if err != nil {
   115→		env.T.Fatalf("CLI command failed: %v\nArgs: %v\nOutput: %s", err, args, output)
   116→	}
   117→	return output
   118→}

   127→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()

   150→// NewRepoEnv creates a TestEnv with an initialized git repo and Entire.
   151→// This is a convenience factory for tests that need a basic repo setup.
   152→func NewRepoEnv(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   153→	t.Helper()

   156→	env.InitEntire(strategy)

   160→// NewRepoWithCommit creates a TestEnv with a git repo, Entire, and an initial commit.
   161→// The initial commit contains a README.md file.
   162→func NewRepoWithCommit(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   163→	t.Helper()
   164→	env := NewRepoEnv(t, strategy)
   165→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository")
   166→	env.GitAdd("README.md")

   169→}
   170→
   171→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates a TestEnv ready for session testing.
   172→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
   173→// and checks out a feature branch. This is the most common setup
   174→// for session and rewind tests since Entire tracking skips main/master.
   175→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
   176→	t.Helper()
   177→	env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strategyName)
   178→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/test-branch")
   179→	return env
   180→}
   181→
   182→// AllStrategies returns all strategy names for parameterized tests.
   183→func AllStrategies() []string {
   184→	return []string{
   185→		strategy.StrategyNameAutoCommit,
   186→		strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit,
   187→	}
   188→}
   189→
   190→// RunForAllStrategies runs a test function for each strategy in parallel.
   191→// This reduces boilerplate for tests that need to verify behavior across all strategies.
   192→// Each subtest gets its own TestEnv with a feature branch ready for testing.
   193→func RunForAllStrategies(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   194→	t.Helper()
   195→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   196→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   197→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   198→			t.Parallel()
   199→			env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strat)
   200→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   201→		})
   202→	}
   203→}
   204→
   205→// RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   206→// using NewRepoWithCommit instead of NewFeatureBranchEnv. Use this for tests
   207→// that need to test behavior on the main branch.
   208→func RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   209→	t.Helper()
   210→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   211→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   212→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   213→			t.Parallel()
   214→			env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strat)
   215→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   216→		})
   217→	}
   218→}
   219→
   220→// RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   221→// using NewRepoEnv (git repo + entire init, no commits). Use this for tests
   222→// that need to verify basic initialization behavior.
   223→func RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   224→	t.Helper()
   225→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   226→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   227→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   228→			t.Parallel()
   229→			env := NewRepoEnv(t, strat)
   230→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   231→		})
   232→	}
   233→}
   234→
   235→// RunForStrategiesSequential runs a test function for specific strategies sequentially.
   236→// Use this for tests that cannot be parallelized (e.g., tests using os.Chdir).
   237→// The strategies parameter allows testing a subset of strategies.
   238→func RunForStrategiesSequential(t *testing.T, strategies []string, testFn func(t *testing.T, strategyName string)) {
   239→	t.Helper()
   240→	for _, strat := range strategies {
   241→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   242→			testFn(t, strat)
   243→		})
   244→	}
   245→}

   247→// InitRepo initializes a git repository in the test environment.
   248→func (env *TestEnv) InitRepo() {
   249→	env.T.Helper()
   250→
   251→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(env.RepoDir, false)
   252→	if err != nil {
   253→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to init git repo: %v", err)
   254→	}
   255→
   256→	// Configure git user for commits
   257→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   258→	if err != nil {
   259→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   260→	}
   261→	cfg.User.Name = "Test User"
   262→	cfg.User.Email = "test@example.com"
   263→	if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
   264→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
   265→	}
   266→}
   267→
   268→// InitEntire initializes the .entire directory with the specified strategy.
   269→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntire(strategyName string) {
   270→	env.InitEntireWithOptions(strategyName, nil)
   271→}
   272→
   273→// InitEntireWithOptions initializes the .entire directory with the specified strategy and options.
   274→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithOptions(strategyName string, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   275→	env.T.Helper()
   276→	env.initEntireInternal(strategyName, "", strategyOptions)
   277→}
   278→
   279→// InitEntireWithAgent initializes an Entire test environment with a specific agent.
   280→// If agentName is empty, defaults to claude-code.
   281→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithAgent(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName) {
   282→	env.T.Helper()
   283→	env.initEntireInternal(strategyName, agentName, nil)
   284→}
   285→
   286→// InitEntireWithAgentAndOptions initializes Entire with the specified strategy, agent, and options.
   287→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithAgentAndOptions(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   288→	env.T.Helper()
   289→	env.initEntireInternal(strategyName, agentName, strategyOptions)

   291→
   292→// initEntireInternal is the common implementation for InitEntire variants.
   293→func (env *TestEnv) initEntireInternal(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   294→	env.T.Helper()
   295→
   296→	// Create .entire directory structure
   297→	entireDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire")
   298→	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   299→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire directory: %v", err)
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	// Create tmp directory
   303→	tmpDir := filepath.Join(entireDir, "tmp")
   304→	if err := os.MkdirAll(tmpDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   305→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire/tmp directory: %v", err)
   306→	}
   307→
   308→	// Write settings.json
   309→	settings := map[string]any{
   310→		"strategy":  strategyName,
   311→		"local_dev": true, // Use go run for hooks in tests
   312→	}
   313→	// Only add agent if specified (otherwise defaults to claude-code)
   314→	if agentName != "" {
   315→		settings["agent"] = string(agentName)
   316→	}
   317→	if strategyOptions != nil {
   318→		settings["strategy_options"] = strategyOptions
   319→	}
   320→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   321→	if err != nil {
   322→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal settings: %v", err)
   323→	}
   324→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(entireDir, paths.SettingsFileName)
   325→	if err := os.WriteFile(settingsPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   326→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write %s: %v", paths.SettingsFileName, err)
   327→	}
   328→}
   329→
   330→// WriteFile creates a file with the given content in the test repo.
   331→// It creates parent directories as needed.
   332→func (env *TestEnv) WriteFile(path, content string) {
   333→	env.T.Helper()
   334→
   335→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   336→
   337→	// Create parent directories
   338→	dir := filepath.Dir(fullPath)
   339→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
   340→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create directory %s: %v", dir, err)
   341→	}
   342→
   343→	if err := os.WriteFile(fullPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   344→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write file %s: %v", path, err)
   345→	}
   346→}

   514→			When:  time.Now(),

   539→// GetGitLog returns a list of commit hashes from HEAD.
   540→func (env *TestEnv) GetGitLog() []string {
   541→	env.T.Helper()
   542→
   543→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   544→	if err != nil {
   545→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   546→	}
   547→
   548→	head, err := repo.Head()
   549→	if err != nil {
   550→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   551→	}
   552→
   553→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   554→	if err != nil {
   555→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get log: %v", err)
   556→	}
   557→
   558→	var commits []string
   559→	err = commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   560→		commits = append(commits, c.Hash.String())
   561→		return nil
   562→	})
   563→	if err != nil {
   564→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   565→	}
   566→
   567→	return commits
   568→}

   570→// GitCheckoutNewBranch creates and checks out a new branch.
   571→// Uses git CLI instead of go-git to work around go-git v5 bug where Checkout
   572→// deletes untracked files (see https://github.com/go-git/go-git/issues/970).
   573→func (env *TestEnv) GitCheckoutNewBranch(branchName string) {
   574→	env.T.Helper()
   575→
   576→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "checkout", "-b", branchName)
   577→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   578→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {

   583→// GetCurrentBranch returns the current branch name.
   584→func (env *TestEnv) GetCurrentBranch() string {
   585→	env.T.Helper()
   586→
   587→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   588→	if err != nil {
   589→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   590→	}
   591→
   592→	head, err := repo.Head()
   593→	if err != nil {
   594→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   595→	}
   596→
   597→	if !head.Name().IsBranch() {
   598→		return "" // Detached HEAD
   599→	}
   600→
   601→	return head.Name().Short()
   602→}
   603→
   604→// RewindPoint mirrors strategy.RewindPoint for test assertions.
   605→type RewindPoint struct {
   606→	ID               string
   607→	Message          string
   608→	MetadataDir      string
   609→	Date             time.Time
   610→	IsTaskCheckpoint bool
   611→	ToolUseID        string
   612→	IsLogsOnly       bool
   613→	CondensationID   string
   614→}
   615→
   616→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   617→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   618→	env.T.Helper()
   619→
   620→	// Run rewind --list using the shared binary
   621→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   622→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   623→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   624→
   625→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   626→	if err != nil {
   627→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   628→	}
   629→
   630→	// Parse JSON output
   631→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   632→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   633→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   634→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   635→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   636→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   637→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   638→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   639→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   640→	}
   641→
   642→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   643→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   644→	}
   645→
   646→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   647→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   648→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   649→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   650→			ID:               jp.ID,
   651→			Message:          jp.Message,
   652→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   653→			Date:             date,
   654→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   655→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   656→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   657→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   658→		}
   659→	}
   660→
   661→	return points
   662→}
   663→
   664→// Rewind performs a rewind to the specified commit ID using the CLI.
   665→func (env *TestEnv) Rewind(commitID string) error {
   666→	env.T.Helper()

   673→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   674→	if err != nil {

   677→
   678→	env.T.Logf("Rewind output: %s", output)
   679→	return nil
   680→}

   720→// BranchExists checks if a branch exists in the repository.
   721→func (env *TestEnv) BranchExists(branchName string) bool {
   722→	env.T.Helper()
   723→
   724→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   725→	if err != nil {
   726→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   727→	}
   728→
   729→	refs, err := repo.References()
   730→	if err != nil {
   731→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
   732→	}
   733→
   734→	found := false
   735→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   736→		if ref.Name().Short() == branchName {
   737→			found = true
   738→		}
   739→		return nil
   740→	})
   741→
   742→	return found
   743→}

   745→// GetCommitMessage returns the commit message for the given commit hash.

   762→
   763→// FileExistsInBranch checks if a file exists in a specific branch's tree.
   764→func (env *TestEnv) FileExistsInBranch(branchName, filePath string) bool {
   765→	env.T.Helper()
   766→
   767→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   768→	if err != nil {
   769→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   770→	}
   771→
   772→	// Get the branch reference
   773→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   774→	if err != nil {
   775→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   776→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   777→		if err != nil {
   778→			return false
   779→		}
   780→	}
   781→
   782→	// Get the commit
   783→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   784→	if err != nil {
   785→		return false
   786→	}
   787→
   788→	// Get the tree
   789→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   790→	if err != nil {
   791→		return false
   792→	}
   793→
   794→	// Check if file exists
   795→	_, err = tree.File(filePath)
   796→	return err == nil
   797→}
   798→
   799→// ReadFileFromBranch reads a file's content from a specific branch's tree.
   800→// Returns the content and true if found, empty string and false if not found.
   801→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileFromBranch(branchName, filePath string) (string, bool) {
   802→	env.T.Helper()
   803→
   804→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   805→	if err != nil {
   806→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   807→	}
   808→
   809→	// Get the branch reference
   810→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   811→	if err != nil {
   812→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   813→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   814→		if err != nil {
   815→			return "", false
   816→		}
   817→	}
   818→
   819→	// Get the commit
   820→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   821→	if err != nil {
   822→		return "", false
   823→	}
   824→
   825→	// Get the tree
   826→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   827→	if err != nil {
   828→		return "", false
   829→	}
   830→
   831→	// Get the file
   832→	file, err := tree.File(filePath)
   833→	if err != nil {
   834→		return "", false
   835→	}
   836→
   837→	// Get the content
   838→	content, err := file.Contents()
   839→	if err != nil {
   840→		return "", false
   841→	}
   842→
   843→	return content, true
   844→}

   846→// GetLatestCommitMessageOnBranch returns the commit message of the latest commit on the given branch.

   869→
   870→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, simulating the prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks.
   871→// This is used for testing manual-commit strategy which needs:
   872→// - prepare-commit-msg hook: adds the Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   873→// - post-commit hook: condenses session data if trailer is present
   874→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   875→	env.T.Helper()
   876→
   877→	// Stage files using go-git
   878→	for _, file := range files {
   879→		env.GitAdd(file)
   880→	}
   881→
   882→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
   883→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   884→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   885→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   886→	}
   887→
   888→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary
   889→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
   890→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   891→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   892→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   893→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   894→	}
   895→
   896→	// Read the modified message
   897→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   898→	if err != nil {
   899→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   900→	}
   901→
   902→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
   903→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   904→	if err != nil {
   905→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   906→	}
   907→
   908→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   909→	if err != nil {
   910→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   911→	}
   912→
   913→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
   914→		Author: &object.Signature{
   915→			Name:  "Test User",
   916→			Email: "test@example.com",
   917→			When:  time.Now(),
   918→		},
   919→	})
   920→	if err != nil {
   921→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   922→	}
   923→
   924→	// Run post-commit hook using the shared binary
   925→	// This triggers condensation if the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   926→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   927→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   928→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   929→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   930→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   931→	}
   932→}
   933→
   934→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
   935→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
   936→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
   937→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
   938→	env.T.Helper()
   939→
   940→	// Stage files using go-git
   941→	for _, file := range files {
   942→		env.GitAdd(file)
   943→	}
   944→
   945→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
   946→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   947→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   948→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   949→	}
   950→
   951→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary
   952→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
   953→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   954→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   955→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   956→	}
   957→
   958→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
   959→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   960→	if err != nil {
   961→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   962→	}
   963→
   964→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
   965→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
   966→	var cleanedLines []string
   967→	for _, line := range lines {
   968→		// Skip the trailer and the comments about it
   969→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   970→			continue
   971→		}
   972→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
   973→			continue
   974→		}
   975→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
   976→			continue
   977→		}
   978→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
   979→	}
   980→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
   981→
   982→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
   983→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   984→	if err != nil {
   985→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   986→	}
   987→
   988→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   989→	if err != nil {
   990→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   991→	}
   992→
   993→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
   994→		Author: &object.Signature{
   995→			Name:  "Test User",
   996→			Email: "test@example.com",
   997→			When:  time.Now(),
   998→		},
   999→	})
  1000→	if err != nil {
  1001→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
  1002→	}
  1003→
  1004→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
  1005→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1006→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1007→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1008→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1009→	}
  1010→}

  1012→// ListBranchesWithPrefix returns all branches that start with the given prefix.
  1013→func (env *TestEnv) ListBranchesWithPrefix(prefix string) []string {
  1014→	env.T.Helper()
  1015→
  1016→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1017→	if err != nil {
  1018→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1019→	}
  1020→
  1021→	refs, err := repo.References()
  1022→	if err != nil {
  1023→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
  1024→	}
  1025→
  1026→	var branches []string
  1027→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
  1028→		name := ref.Name().Short()
  1029→		if len(name) >= len(prefix) && name[:len(prefix)] == prefix {
  1030→			branches = append(branches, name)
  1031→		}
  1032→		return nil
  1033→	})
  1034→
  1035→	return branches
  1036→}

  1038→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/sessions branch.
  1039→// This is used by tests that previously extracted the checkpoint ID from commit message trailers.
  1040→// Now that active branch commits are clean (no trailers), we get the ID from the sessions branch.
  1041→// Fatals if the checkpoint ID cannot be found, with detailed context about what was found.
  1042→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1043→	env.T.Helper()
  1044→
  1045→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1046→	if err != nil {
  1047→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1048→	}
  1049→
  1050→	// Get the entire/sessions branch
  1051→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1052→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1053→	if err != nil {
  1054→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get %s branch: %v", paths.MetadataBranchName, err)
  1055→	}
  1056→
  1057→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1058→	if err != nil {
  1059→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit: %v", err)
  1060→	}
  1061→
  1062→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1063→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1064→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1065→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1066→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1067→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1068→		}
  1069→	}
  1070→
  1071→	env.T.Fatalf("could not find checkpoint ID in %s branch commit message:\n%s",
  1072→		paths.MetadataBranchName, commit.Message)
  1073→	return ""
  1074→}
  1075→

  1077→// Returns empty string if the branch doesn't exist or has no checkpoint commits yet.

  1079→func (env *TestEnv) TryGetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1080→	env.T.Helper()

  1094→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())

  1098→
  1099→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message

  1101→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {

  1116→// GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage extracts the Entire-Checkpoint trailer from a commit message.
  1117→// Returns empty string if no trailer found.
  1118→func (env *TestEnv) GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitSHA string) string {
  1119→	env.T.Helper()
  1120→
  1121→	msg := env.GetCommitMessage(commitSHA)
  1122→	cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(msg)
  1123→	if !found {
  1124→		return ""
  1125→	}
  1126→	return cpID.String()
  1127→}

  1129→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD on the active branch
  1130→// and returns the checkpoint ID from the first commit that has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
  1131→// This verifies that condensation actually happened (commit has trailer) without relying
  1132→// on timestamp-based matching.
  1133→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() string {
  1134→	env.T.Helper()
  1135→
  1136→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1137→	if err != nil {
  1138→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1139→	}
  1140→
  1141→	head, err := repo.Head()
  1142→	if err != nil {
  1143→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
  1144→	}
  1145→
  1146→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
  1147→	if err != nil {
  1148→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
  1149→	}
  1150→
  1151→	var checkpointID string
  1152→	//nolint:errcheck // ForEach callback handles errors
  1153→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
  1154→		if cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(c.Message); found {
  1155→			checkpointID = cpID.String()
  1156→			return errors.New("stop iteration") // Found it, stop
  1157→		}
  1158→		return nil
  1159→	})
  1160→
  1161→	if checkpointID == "" {
  1162→		env.T.Fatalf("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
  1163→	}
  1164→
  1165→	return checkpointID
  1166→}
  1167→
  1168→// ShardedCheckpointPath returns the sharded path for a checkpoint ID.
  1169→// Format: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>
  1170→// Delegates to id.CheckpointID.Path() for consistency.
  1171→func ShardedCheckpointPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1172→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path()
  1173→}

  1175→func findModuleRoot() string {
  1176→	// Start from this source file's location and walk up to find go.mod
  1177→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
  1178→	if !ok {
  1179→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
  1180→	}
  1181→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
  1182→
  1183→	for {
  1184→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
  1185→			return dir
  1186→		}
  1187→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
  1188→		if parent == dir {
  1189→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
  1190→		}
  1191→		dir = parent
  1192→	}
  1193→}

    60→// SimulateUserPromptSubmitWithResponse simulates the UserPromptSubmit hook

    62→func (r *HookRunner) SimulateUserPromptSubmitWithResponse(sessionID string) (*HookResponse, error) {
    63→	r.T.Helper()

    65→	input := map[string]string{

    89→		}

   180→func (r *HookRunner) runHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   181→	// Run using the shared test binary
   182→	// Command structure: entire hooks claude-code <hook-name>
   183→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   184→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   185→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   186→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   187→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   188→	)
   189→
   190→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   191→	if err != nil {
   192→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   193→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   194→	}
   195→
   196→	r.T.Logf("Hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   197→	return nil
   198→}

   200→// Session represents a simulated Claude Code session.
   201→type Session struct {
   202→	ID                string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "test-session-1")
   203→	EntireID          string // Entire session ID with date prefix (e.g., "2025-12-02-test-session-1")
   204→	TranscriptPath    string
   205→	TranscriptBuilder *TranscriptBuilder
   206→	env               *TestEnv
   207→}
   208→
   209→// FileChange represents a file modification in a session.
   210→type FileChange struct {
   211→	Path    string
   212→	Content string
   213→}
   214→
   215→// NewSession creates a new simulated session.
   216→func (env *TestEnv) NewSession() *Session {
   217→	env.T.Helper()
   218→
   219→	env.SessionCounter++
   220→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("test-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   221→	entireID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   222→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".jsonl")
   223→
   224→	return &Session{
   225→		ID:                sessionID,
   226→		EntireID:          entireID,
   227→		TranscriptPath:    transcriptPath,
   228→		TranscriptBuilder: NewTranscriptBuilder(),
   229→		env:               env,
   230→	}
   231→}
   232→
   233→// CreateTranscript builds and writes a transcript for the session.
   234→func (s *Session) CreateTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   235→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddUserMessage(prompt)
   236→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("I'll help you with that.")
   237→
   238→	for _, change := range changes {
   239→		toolID := s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolUse("mcp__acp__Write", change.Path, change.Content)
   240→		s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolResult(toolID)
   241→	}
   242→
   243→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("Done!")
   244→
   245→	if err := s.TranscriptBuilder.WriteToFile(s.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
   246→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	return s.TranscriptPath
   250→}

   327→// ClearSessionState removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   328→// This simulates what happens when a user commits their changes (session is "completed").
   329→// Used in tests to allow sequential sessions to run without triggering concurrent session warnings.
   330→func (env *TestEnv) ClearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
   331→	env.T.Helper()
   332→
   333→	// Session state is stored in .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
   334→	// Use sessionid.EntireSessionID to get the full session ID with date prefix
   335→	entireSessionID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   336→	stateFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions", entireSessionID+".json")
   337→
   338→	if err := os.Remove(stateFile); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {
   339→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to clear session state: %w", err)
   340→	}
   341→	return nil
   342→}

   351→// runHookWithOutput runs a hook and returns both stdout and stderr separately.

   358→	)

   361→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   362→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr

   364→	err := cmd.Run()
   365→	return HookOutput{

   396→// GetSessionState reads and returns the session state for the given session ID.

   454→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   455→	// Run using the shared test binary
   456→	// Command structure: entire hooks gemini <hook-name>
   457→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "gemini", hookName)
   458→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   459→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   460→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   461→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+r.GeminiProjectDir,
   462→	)
   463→
   464→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   465→	if err != nil {
   466→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   467→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   468→	}
   469→
   470→	r.T.Logf("Gemini hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   471→	return nil
   472→}

   566→// GeminiSession represents a simulated Gemini CLI session.
   567→type GeminiSession struct {
   568→	ID             string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "gemini-session-1")
   569→	EntireID       string // Entire session ID with date prefix (e.g., "2025-12-02-gemini-session-1")
   570→	TranscriptPath string
   571→	env            *TestEnv
   572→}
   573→
   574→// NewGeminiSession creates a new simulated Gemini session.
   575→func (env *TestEnv) NewGeminiSession() *GeminiSession {
   576→	env.T.Helper()
   577→
   578→	env.SessionCounter++
   579→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("gemini-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   580→	entireID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   581→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".json")
   582→
   583→	return &GeminiSession{
   584→		ID:             sessionID,
   585→		EntireID:       entireID,
   586→		TranscriptPath: transcriptPath,
   587→		env:            env,
   588→	}
   589→}
   590→
   591→// CreateGeminiTranscript creates a Gemini JSON transcript file for the session.
   592→func (s *GeminiSession) CreateGeminiTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   593→	// Build Gemini-format transcript (JSON, not JSONL)
   594→	messages := []map[string]interface{}{
   595→		{
   596→			"type":    "user",
   597→			"content": prompt,
   598→		},
   599→		{
   600→			"type":    "assistant",
   601→			"content": "I'll help you with that.",
   602→		},
   603→	}
   604→
   605→	for _, change := range changes {
   606→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   607→			"type": "tool_use",
   608→			"name": "write_file",
   609→			"input": map[string]string{
   610→				"path":    change.Path,
   611→				"content": change.Content,
   612→			},
   613→		})
   614→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   615→			"type":   "tool_result",
   616→			"output": "File written successfully",
   617→		})
   618→	}
   619→
   620→	messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   621→		"type":    "assistant",
   622→		"content": "Done!",
   623→	})
   624→
   625→	transcript := map[string]interface{}{
   626→		"sessionId": s.ID,
   627→		"messages":  messages,
   628→	}
   629→
   630→	// Ensure directory exists
   631→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(s.TranscriptPath), 0o755); err != nil {
   632→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to create transcript dir: %v", err)
   633→	}
   634→
   635→	// Write transcript
   636→	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(transcript, "", "  ")
   637→	if err != nil {
   638→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal transcript: %v", err)
   639→	}
   640→	if err := os.WriteFile(s.TranscriptPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   641→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   642→	}
   643→
   644→	return s.TranscriptPath
   645→}

Claude can run `git rebase` via the Bash tool\n// 2. No new prompt is submitted between the rebase and the next checkpoint\n// 3. Without migration in SaveChanges, checkpoints go to an orphaned shadow branch\n//\n// The test validates that:\n// - Checkpoints before rebase go to the original shadow branch\n// - Checkpoints after rebase go to the new (migrated) shadow branch\n// - The session state's BaseCommit is updated correctly\nfunc TestShadow_MidSessionRebaseMigration(t *testing.T) {\n\tenv := NewTestEnv(t)\n\tdefer env.Cleanup()\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 1: Setup - Create commits to rebase onto\n\t// ========================================\n\tenv.InitRepo()\n\n\t// Create initial commit on main\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"README.md\", \"# Test Repository\")\n\tenv.GitAdd(\"README.md\")\n\tenv.GitCommit(\"Initial commit\")\n\n\t// Create a second commit on main (this will be our rebase target)\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"base.txt\", \"base content\")\n\tenv.GitAdd(\"base.txt\")\n\tenv.GitCommit(\"Add base file\")\n\tmainHead := env.GetHeadHash()\n\n\t// Create feature branch from initial commit (before base.txt)\n\tenv.gitCheckout(\"HEAD~1\")\n\tenv.GitCheckoutNewBranch(\"feature/rebase-test\")\n\n\t// Initialize Entire after branch creation\n\tenv.InitEntire(strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)\n\n\t// Create a commit on feature branch\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"feature.txt\", \"feature content\")\n\tenv.GitAdd(\"feature.txt\")\n\tenv.GitCommit(\"Add feature file\")\n\n\tinitialFeatureHead := env.GetHeadHash()\n\tt.Logf(\"Initial feature HEAD: %s\", initialFeatureHead[:7])\n\tt.Logf(\"Main HEAD (rebase target): %s\", mainHead[:7])\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 2: Start session and create first checkpoint\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 2: Starting session and creating first checkpoint\")\n\n\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\tif err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Create first file change\n\tfileAContent := \"package main\\n\\nfunc A() {}\\n\"\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"a.go\", fileAContent)\n\n\tsession.CreateTranscript(\n\t\t\"Create function A\",\n\t\t[]FileChange{{Path: \"a.go\", Content: fileAContent}},\n\t)\n\tif err := env.SimulateStop(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateStop (checkpoint 1) failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify shadow branch exists at original commit\n\toriginalShadowBranch := \"entire/\" + initialFeatureHead[:7]\n\tif !env.BranchExists(originalShadowBranch) {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Shadow branch %s should exist after first checkpoint\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t}\n\tt.Logf(\"Checkpoint 1 created on shadow branch: %s\", originalShadowBranch)\n\n\t// Verify 1 rewind point\n\tpoints := env.GetRewindPoints()\n\tif len(points) != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Expected 1 rewind point after first checkpoint, got %d\", len(points))\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 3: Simulate Claude doing a rebase (via Bash tool)\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 3: Simulating Claude performing rebase via Bash tool\")\n\n\t// This simulates what happens when Claude runs: git rebase main\n\t// Note: We're NOT calling SimulateUserPromptSubmit here because the rebase\n\t// happens mid-session as part of Claude's tool execution\n\tcmd := exec.Command(\"git\", \"rebase\", \"main\")\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"git rebase failed: %v\\nOutput: %s\", err, output)\n\t}\n\n\tnewFeatureHead := env.GetHeadHash()\n\tt.Logf(\"After rebase, feature HEAD: %s (was: %s)\", newFeatureHead[:7], initialFeatureHead[:7])\n\n\t// Verify HEAD actually changed (rebase happened)\n\tif newFeatureHead == initialFeatureHead {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"HEAD should have changed after rebase\")\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 4: Create second checkpoint AFTER rebase (without new prompt)\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 4: Creating checkpoint after rebase (no new prompt submit)\")\n\n\t// Claude continues working after the rebase - creates more files\n\t// Note: We do NOT call SimulateUserPromptSubmit because this is continuing\n\t// the same tool execution flow (no new user prompt)\n\tfileBContent := \"package main\\n\\nfunc B() {}\\n\"\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"b.go\", fileBContent)\n\n\t// Reset transcript builder for new checkpoint\n\tsession.TranscriptBuilder = NewTranscriptBuilder()\n\tsession.CreateTranscript(\n\t\t\"Create and migrate the shadow branch\n\tif err := env.SimulateStop(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateStop (checkpoint 2 after rebase) failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 5: Verify migration happened\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 5: Verifying shadow branch migration\")\n\n\t// The {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"New shadow branch %s should exist after migration\", newShadowBranch)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"Available branches: %v\", env.ListBranchesWithPrefix(\"entire/\"))\n\n\t\t// Check if old shadow branch still exists (would indicate no migration)\n\t\tif env.BranchExists(originalShadowBranch) {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"Original shadow branch %s still exists - migration did not happen!\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ New shadow branch exists: %s\", newShadowBranch)\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify old shadow branch is gone (renamed/migrated)\n\tif env.BranchExists(originalShadowBranch) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"Original shadow branch %s should have been deleted after migration\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ Original shadow branch %s was cleaned up\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify session state has updated BaseCommit\n\tstate, err := env.GetSessionState(session.ID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"GetSessionState failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif state == nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"Session state should exist\")\n\t}\n\tif state.BaseCommit != newFeatureHead {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"Session BaseCommit should be %s (rebased HEAD), got %s\",\n\t\t\tnewFeatureHead[:7], state.BaseCommit[:7])\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ Session BaseCommit updated to: %s\", state.BaseCommit[:7])\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify we have 2 checkpoints (both should be on the new shadow branch)\n\tpoints = env.GetRewindPoints()\n\tif len(points) != 2 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"Expected 2 rewind points, got %d\", len(points))\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ Found %d rewind points after migration\", len(points))\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 6: Verify rewind still works\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 6: Verifying rewind still works after migration\")\n\n\t// Find checkpoint 1 (before rebase)\n\tvar checkpoint1ID string\n\tfor _, p := range points {\n\t\tif p.Message == \"Create function A\" {\n\t\t\tcheckpoint1ID = p.ID\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif checkpoint1ID == \"\" {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Could not find checkpoint 1 in rewind points\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Rewind to checkpoint 1\n\tif err := env.Rewind(checkpoint1ID); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Rewind to checkpoint 1 failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify b.go is gone (it was created after checkpoint 1)\n\tif env.FileExists(\"b.go\") {\n\t\tt.Error(\"b.go should NOT exist after rewind to checkpoint 1\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify a.go exists\n\tif !env.FileExists(\"a.go\") {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a.go should exist after rewind to checkpoint 1\")\n\t}\n\n\tt.Log(\"Mid-session rebase migration test completed successfully!\")\n}\n\n// gitCheckout is a helper to checkout a specific ref using git CLI.\n// Uses CLI instead of go-git to work around go-git v5 bug with untracked files.\nfunc (env *TestEnv) gitCheckout(ref string) {\n\tenv.T.Helper()\n\n\tcmd := exec.Command(\"git\", \"checkout\", ref)\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tenv.T.Fatalf(\"git checkout %s failed: %v\\nOutput: %s\", ref, err, output)\n\t}\n}\n"

> AGENT

The test confirms the bug:

1. **Checkpoint went to wrong branch**: `entire/6c0451c` (old) instead of `entire/14a1b07` (new)
2. **No migration happened**: The old shadow branch still exists
3. **BaseCommit not updated**: Still shows old commit
4. **Rewind broken**: No rewind points found (they're orphaned on the old branch)

Now let's implement the fix. I'll add the migration logic to `SaveChanges`.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go",
  "content": "package strategy\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n\n\t\"entire.io/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint\"\n\n\t\"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5\"\n\t\"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing\"\n)\n\n// migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded checks if HEAD has changed since the session started\n// and migrates the shadow branch to the new base commit if needed.\n//\n// This handles the scenario where Claude performs a rebase, pull, or other git operation\n// that changes HEAD mid-session (via a tool call), without a new prompt being submitted.\n// Without this migration, checkpoints would be saved to an orphaned shadow branch.\n//\n// Returns true if migration occurred, false otherwise.\nfunc (s *ManualCommitStrategy) migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {\n\tif state == nil || state.BaseCommit == \"\" {\n\t\treturn false, nil\n\t}\n\n\thead, err := repo.Head()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to get HEAD: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tcurrentHead := head.Hash().String()\n\tif state.BaseCommit == currentHead {\n\t\treturn false, nil // No migration needed\n\t}\n\n\t// HEAD changed - check if old shadow branch exists and migrate it\n\toldShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)\n\tnewShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(currentHead)\n\n\toldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)\n\toldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t// Old shadow branch doesn't exist - just update state.BaseCommit\n\t\t// This can happen if this is the first checkpoint after HEAD changed\n\t\tstate.BaseCommit = currentHead\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Updated session base commit to %s (HEAD changed during session)\\n\", currentHead[:7])\n\t\treturn true, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit\n\tnewRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)\n\n\t// Create new reference pointing to same commit as old shadow branch\n\tnewRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())\n\tif err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w\", newShadowBranch, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Delete old reference\n\tif err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {\n\t\t// Non-fatal: log but continue - the important thing is the new branch exists\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\\n\", oldShadowBranch, err)\n\t}\n\n\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Moved shadow branch from %s to %s (HEAD changed during session)\\n\",\n\t\toldShadowBranch, newShadowBranch)\n\n\t// Update state with new base commit\n\tstate.BaseCommit = currentHead\n\treturn true, nil\n}\n"
}

    12→
    13→// migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded checks if HEAD has changed since the session started
    14→// and migrates the shadow branch to the new base commit if needed.
    15→//
    16→// This handles the scenario where Claude performs a rebase, pull, or other git operation
    17→// that changes HEAD mid-session (via a tool call), without a new prompt being submitted.
    18→// Without this migration, checkpoints would be saved to an orphaned shadow branch.
    19→//
    20→// Returns true if migration occurred, false otherwise.
    21→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {
    22→	if state == nil || state.BaseCommit == "" {
    23→		return false, nil
    24→	}
    25→
    26→	head, err := repo.Head()
    27→	if err != nil {
    28→		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
    29→	}
    30→
    31→	currentHead := head.Hash().String()
    32→	if state.BaseCommit == currentHead {
    33→		return false, nil // No migration needed
    34→	}
    35→
    36→	// HEAD changed - check if old shadow branch exists and migrate it
    37→	oldShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)
    38→	newShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(currentHead)
    39→
    40→	oldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)
    41→	oldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true)
    42→	if err != nil {
    43→		// Old shadow branch doesn't exist - just update state.BaseCommit
    44→		// This can happen if this is the first checkpoint after HEAD changed
    45→		state.BaseCommit = currentHead
    46→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Updated session base commit to %s (HEAD changed during session)\n", currentHead[:7])
    47→		return true, nil
    48→	}
    49→
    50→	// Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit
    51→	newRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)
    52→
    53→	// Create new reference pointing to same commit as old shadow branch
    54→	newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())
    55→	if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {
    56→		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w", newShadowBranch, err)
    57→	}
    58→
    59→	// Delete old reference
    60→	if err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {
    61→		// Non-fatal: log but continue - the important thing is the new branch exists
    62→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\n", oldShadowBranch, err)
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Moved shadow branch from %s to %s (HEAD changed during session)\n",
    66→		oldShadowBranch, newShadowBranch)
    67→
    68→	// Update state with new base commit
    69→	state.BaseCommit = currentHead
    70→	return true, nil
    71→}
    72→

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's a summary of the fix:

## Summary

### The Bug
When Claude performs a rebase (or any git operation that changes HEAD) mid-session via a tool call, checkpoints were being saved to an **orphaned shadow branch** based on the old `state.BaseCommit`. Migration only happened on new prompt submission (`InitializeSession`), but not in `SaveChanges`.

### The Fix

1. **Created `manual_commit_migration.go`** - New helper function `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` that:
   - Compares current HEAD to `state.BaseCommit`
   - If different, migrates the shadow branch (rename from old hash to new hash)
   - Updates `state.BaseCommit`

2. **Modified `manual_commit_git.go`** - Added migration check to both:
   - `SaveChanges()` - for regular checkpoints
   - `SaveTaskCheckpoint()` - for subagent checkpoints

3. **Created integration test** `mid_session_rebase_test.go` - Tests the scenario:
   - Session starts at commit A
   - First checkpoint saved to `entire/A`
   - Claude does `git rebase` (HEAD changes to B)
   - Second checkpoint triggers migration and saves to `entire/B`
   - Verifies old branch is cleaned up, state is updated, and rewind works

### Files Changed
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` - Added migration calls
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go` - New file with migration logic
- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/mid_session_rebase_test.go` - New integration test

> DEVELOPER

would this also have been an issue with claude switching branches or doing an reset?

> AGENT

Yes, the same bug would have affected any git operation that changes HEAD mid-session:

| Operation | Changes HEAD? | Would have been affected? |
|-----------|--------------|---------------------------|
| `git rebase` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git checkout <branch>` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git checkout <commit>` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git reset --hard <ref>` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git merge` (with commit) | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git cherry-pick` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git pull` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `git commit --amend` | ✓ | ✓ |

The fix handles all of these because it simply compares:
```go
if state.BaseCommit != currentHead {
    // Migrate shadow branch
}
```

Any operation that moves HEAD triggers migration.

**However**, there's a subtle difference worth noting:

- **Rebase/reset/amend**: Rewrites history on the same branch - migration makes sense
- **Branch switch**: Moves to completely different history - might be unexpected behavior

If Claude switches from `feature-a` to `feature-b`, the fix will migrate the shadow branch to `feature-b`'s HEAD. This means:
- Checkpoints from both branches end up linked together
- Rewind could restore files from a completely different branch context

This might be fine (Claude chose to switch), but it could also be confusing. Should I add a test case for branch switching to document this behavior, or would you prefer different handling for that scenario (e.g., warn or start a new session)?

> DEVELOPER

can we check that there is not a race condition when we would cleanup the shadow branch in theory and then a rebase happening? both done by claude in a single prompt execution?

> AGENT

Good question. Let me trace through that scenario and write a test to verify the behavior.

**Potential problematic scenario:**
1. Session at commit A, checkpoints on `entire/A`
2. Claude runs `git commit` → creates commit B, triggers post-commit hook (condensation)
3. Claude runs `git rebase` → HEAD moves to C
4. SaveChanges called → migration tries to find `entire/A`

The question is: does condensation delete the shadow branch? Let me check.

   551→// filterSessionsWithNewContent returns sessions that have new transcript content
   552→// beyond what was already condensed.
   553→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) filterSessionsWithNewContent(repo *git.Repository, sessions []*SessionState) []*SessionState {
   554→	var result []*SessionState

   550→
   551→// filterSessionsWithNewContent returns sessions that have new transcript content
   552→// beyond what was already condensed.
   553→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) filterSessionsWithNewContent(repo *git.Repository, sessions []*SessionState) []*SessionState {
   554→	var result []*SessionState
   555→
   556→	for _, state := range sessions {
   557→		hasNew, err := s.sessionHasNewContent(repo, state)
   558→		if err != nil {
   559→			// On error, include the session (fail open for hooks)
   560→			result = append(result, state)
   561→			continue
   562→		}
   563→		if hasNew {
   564→			result = append(result, state)
   565→		}
   566→	}
   567→
   568→	return result
   569→}
   570→
   571→// sessionHasNewContent checks if a session has new transcript content
   572→// beyond what was already condensed.
   573→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContent(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {
   574→	// Get shadow branch
   575→	shadowBranchName := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)
   576→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   577→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   578→	if err != nil {
   579→		// No shadow branch means no Stop has happened since the last condensation.
   580→		// However, the agent may have done work (including commits) without a Stop.
   581→		// Check the live transcript to detect this scenario.
   582→		return s.sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript(repo, state)
   583→	}
   584→
   585→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   586→	if err != nil {
   587→		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit object: %w", err)
   588→	}
   589→
   590→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   591→	if err != nil {
   592→		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit tree: %w", err)
   593→	}
   594→
   595→	// Look for transcript file
   596→	metadataDir := paths.EntireMetadataDir + "/" + state.SessionID
   597→	var transcriptLines int
   598→
   599→	if file, fileErr := tree.File(metadataDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileName); fileErr == nil {
   600→		if content, contentErr := file.Contents(); contentErr == nil {
   601→			transcriptLines = countTranscriptLines(content)
   602→		}
   603→	} else if file, fileErr := tree.File(metadataDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileNameLegacy); fileErr == nil {
   604→		if content, contentErr := file.Contents(); contentErr == nil {
   605→			transcriptLines = countTranscriptLines(content)
   606→		}
   607→	}
   608→
   609→	// Has new content if there are more lines than already condensed
   610→	return transcriptLines > state.CondensedTranscriptLines, nil
   611→}
   612→
   613→// countTranscriptLines counts lines in a transcript, matching the counting method used
   614→// in extractSessionData for consistency. This trims trailing empty lines (from final \n
   615→// in JSONL) but includes empty lines in the middle of the file.
   616→func countTranscriptLines(content string) int {
   617→	lines := strings.Split(content, "\n")
   618→	// Trim trailing empty lines (from final \n in JSONL)
   619→	for len(lines) > 0 && strings.TrimSpace(lines[len(lines)-1]) == "" {
   620→		lines = lines[:len(lines)-1]
   621→	}
   622→	return len(lines)
   623→}
   624→

err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Create file and checkpoint\n\tfileAContent := \"package main\\n\\nfunc A() {}\\n\"\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"a.go\", fileAContent)\n\n\tsession.CreateTranscript(\n\t\t\"Create function A\",\n\t\t[]FileChange{{Path: \"a.go\", Content: fileAContent}},\n\t)\n\tif err := env.SimulateStop(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateStop (checkpoint 1) failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify shadow branch exists\n\toriginalShadowBranch := \"entire/\" + initialFeatureHead[:7]\n\tif !env.BranchExists(originalShadowBranch) {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Shadow branch %s should exist after first checkpoint\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t}\n\tt.Logf(\"Checkpoint 1 created on shadow branch: %s\", originalShadowBranch)\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 3: Claude commits (triggers condensation which DELETES shadow branch)\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 3: Claude commits (triggers condensation)\")\n\n\t// Stage and commit the file\n\tenv.GitAdd(\"a.go\")\n\t// Use GitCommitWithShadowHooks to simulate the full commit flow with hooks\n\tenv.GitCommitWithShadowHooks(\"Add function A\", \"a.go\")\n\n\tpostCommitHead := env.GetHeadHash()\n\tt.Logf(\"After commit, feature HEAD: %s\", postCommitHead[:7])\n\n\t// Verify shadow branch was deleted by condensation\n\tif env.BranchExists(originalShadowBranch) {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"Note: Shadow branch %s still exists after commit (condensation may not have deleted it)\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ Shadow branch %s was deleted by condensation\", originalShadowBranch)\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify data was condensed to entire/sessions\n\tif !env.BranchExists(\"entire/sessions\") {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"entire/sessions branch should exist after condensation\")\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 4: Claude rebases (HEAD changes again)\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 4: Claude rebases onto master\")\n\n\tcmd := exec.Command(\"git\", \"rebase\", \"master\")\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"git rebase failed: %v\\nOutput: %s\", err, output)\n\t}\n\n\tpostRebaseHead := env.GetHeadHash()\n\tt.Logf(\"After rebase, feature HEAD: %s (was: %s)\", postRebaseHead[:7], postCommitHead[:7])\n\n\t// Verify HEAD changed\n\tif postRebaseHead == postCommitHead {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"HEAD should have changed after rebase\")\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 5: Create another checkpoint after commit+rebase\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 5: Creating checkpoint after commit and rebase\")\n\n\tfileBContent := \"package main\\n\\nfunc B() {}\\n\"\n\tenv.WriteFile(\"b.go\", fileBContent)\n\n\t// Reset transcript for new checkpoint\n\tsession.TranscriptBuilder = NewTranscriptBuilder()\n\tsession.CreateTranscript(\n\t\t\"Create function B after rebase\",\n\t\t[]FileChange{{Path: \"b.go\", Content: fileBContent}},\n\t)\n\n\t// This should NOT fail even though:\n\t// - Original shadow branch was deleted by condensation\n\t// - HEAD changed twice (commit, then rebase)\n\t// - Session state still has old BaseCommit\n\tif err := env.SimulateStop(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateStop (checkpoint 2 after commit+rebase) failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// ========================================\n\t// Phase 6: Verify correct behavior\n\t// ========================================\n\tt.Log(\"Phase 6: Verifying correct behavior\")\n\n\t// New shadow branch should exist (based on rebased HEAD)\n\tnewShadowBranch := \"entire/\" + postRebaseHead[:7]\n\tif !env.BranchExists(newShadowBranch) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"New shadow branch %s should exist\", newShadowBranch)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"Available branches: %v\", env.ListBranchesWithPrefix(\"entire/\"))\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ New shadow branch exists: %s\", newShadowBranch)\n\t}\n\n\t// Session state should have updated BaseCommit\n\tstate, err := env.GetSessionState(session.ID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"GetSessionState failed: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif state == nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"Session state should exist\")\n\t}\n\tif state.BaseCommit != postRebaseHead {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"Session BaseCommit should be %s (rebased HEAD), got %s\",\n\t\t\tpostRebaseHead[:7], state.BaseCommit[:7])\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ Session BaseCommit updated to: %s\", state.BaseCommit[:7])\n\t}\n\n\t// Should have rewind points (at least the new checkpoint)\n\tpoints := env.GetRewindPoints()\n\tif len(points) == 0 {\n\t\tt.Error(\"Expected at least 1 rewind point\")\n\t} else {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"✓ Found %d rewind point(s)\", len(points))\n\t}\n\n\tt.Log(\"Commit-then-rebase mid-session test completed successfully!\")\n}"

{

## Summary

**No race condition** - The fix handles all scenarios correctly:

### Scenario 1: Rebase mid-session (original bug)
- Migration detects HEAD change, renames shadow branch, continues

### Scenario 2: Commit then rebase mid-session
- Commit triggers condensation → shadow branch deleted
- Rebase changes HEAD again
- Migration detects HEAD change, but old shadow branch is gone
- Code handles this gracefully: just updates `state.BaseCommit` and creates fresh shadow branch

### Why there's no race condition
1. **Git hooks are synchronous** - when Claude runs `git commit`, the post-commit hook runs to completion before Claude's next command
2. **Sequential execution** - commit → condensation → branch deleted → rebase → SaveChanges
3. **Graceful handling** - if shadow branch doesn't exist, the migration just updates `state.BaseCommit`

### Files changed
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` - Added migration calls in `SaveChanges` and `SaveTaskCheckpoint`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go` - New file with `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` helper
- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/mid_session_rebase_test.go` - Two new integration tests:
  - `TestShadow_MidSessionRebaseMigration` - Basic rebase scenario
  - `TestShadow_CommitThenRebaseMidSession` - Commit + rebase scenario (verifies no race condition)

> DEVELOPER

This migration logic duplicates similar code in manual_commit_hooks.go at lines 876-909 (in the InitializeSession function). Both implementations handle the same scenario: detecting HEAD changes and migrating shadow branches.

Consider extracting this into a shared helper function to avoid maintenance issues. The logic is nearly identical:

Check if HEAD matches state.BaseCommit
If not, check if old shadow branch exists
If it exists, create new shadow branch pointing to same commit
Remove old shadow branch
Update state.BaseCommit
The main difference is the user-facing message text ("HEAD changed during session" vs "base commit changed after pull/rebase"), but the core logic is the same. Having two implementations increases the risk of divergence and bugs when fixing issues in one but not the other.

  "content": "package strategy\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n\n\t\"entire.io/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint\"\n\n\t\"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5\"\n\t\"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing\"\n)\n\n// MigrationResult describes what happened during shadow branch migration.\ntype MigrationResult struct {\n\t// Migrated is true if the BaseCommit was updated (HEAD changed)\n\tMigrated bool\n\t// BranchMoved is true if a shadow branch was renamed (not just BaseCommit updated)\n\tBranchMoved bool\n\t// OldBaseCommit is the previous BaseCommit (only set if Migrated is true)\n\tOldBaseCommit string\n\t// NewBaseCommit is the new BaseCommit (only set if Migrated is true)\n\tNewBaseCommit string\n\t// OldBranch is the old shadow branch name (only set if BranchMoved is true)\n\tOldBranch string\n\t// NewBranch is the new shadow branch name (only set if BranchMoved is true)\n\tNewBranch string\n}\n\n// migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded checks if HEAD has changed since the session started\n// and migrates the shadow branch to the new base commit if needed.\n//\n// This handles the scenario where HEAD changes mid-session (via rebase, pull,\n// commit, checkout, etc.) without a new prompt being submitted.\n// Without this migration, checkpoints would be saved to an orphaned shadow branch.\n//\n// The function does NOT print any messages - callers should use the returned\n// MigrationResult to print appropriate context-specific messages.\nfunc (s *ManualCommitStrategy) migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (MigrationResult, error) {\n\tresult := MigrationResult{}\n\n\tif state == nil || state.BaseCommit == \"\" {\n\t\treturn result, nil\n\t}\n\n\thead, err := repo.Head()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn result, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to get HEAD: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tcurrentHead := head.Hash().String()\n\tif state.BaseCommit == currentHead {\n\t\treturn result, nil // No migration needed\n\t}\n\n\t// HEAD changed - record the change\n\tresult.Migrated = true\n\tresult.OldBaseCommit = state.BaseCommit\n\tresult.NewBaseCommit = currentHead\n\n\t// Check if old shadow branch exists and migrate it\n\toldShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit)\n\tnewShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(currentHead)\n\n\toldRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch)\n\toldRef, err := repo.Reference(oldRefName, true)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t// Old shadow branch doesn't exist - just update state.BaseCommit\n\t\t// This can happen if:\n\t\t// - First checkpoint after HEAD changed\n\t\t// - Shadow branch was deleted by condensation\n\t\tstate.BaseCommit = currentHead\n\t\treturn result, nil //nolint:nilerr // err is \"reference not found\" which is fine\n\t}\n\n\t// Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit\n\tresult.BranchMoved = true\n\tresult.OldBranch = oldShadowBranch\n\tresult.NewBranch = newShadowBranch\n\n\tnewRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)\n\n\t// Create new reference pointing to same commit as old shadow branch\n\tnewRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())\n\tif err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {\n\t\treturn result, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w\", newShadowBranch, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Delete old reference\n\tif err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {\n\t\t// Non-fatal: log but continue - the important thing is the new branch exists\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\\n\", oldShadowBranch, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Update state with new base commit\n\tstate.BaseCommit = currentHead\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n\n// printMigrationMessage prints a user-facing message about shadow branch migration.\n// The reason parameter describes why the migration happened (e.g., \"during session\", \"after pull/rebase\").\nfunc printMigrationMessage(result MigrationResult, reason string) {\n\tif !result.Migrated {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif result.BranchMoved {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Moved shadow branch from %s to %s (%s)\\n\",\n\t\t\tresult.OldBranch, result.NewBranch, reason)\n\t}\n\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Updated session base commit to %s\\n\", result.NewBaseCommit[:7])\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "\t\t// Check if HEAD has moved (user pulled/rebased or committed) and migrate if needed\n\t\tmigrationResult, err := s.migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo, state)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to check/migrate shadow branch: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif migrationResult.Migrated {\n\t\t\tprintMigrationMessage(migrationResult, \"base commit changed after pull/rebase\")\n\t\t\tneedSave = true\n\t\t}"
}

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's a summary of the refactor:

## Summary

**Extracted shared migration logic** into `manual_commit_migration.go`:

1. **`MigrationResult` struct** - Returns detailed information about what happened:
   - `Migrated` - Whether BaseCommit was updated
   - `BranchMoved` - Whether a shadow branch was renamed
   - Old/new commit hashes and branch names

2. **`migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()`** - Shared helper that:
   - Checks if HEAD differs from `state.BaseCommit`
   - Migrates shadow branch if it exists
   - Updates `state.BaseCommit`
   - Does NOT print messages (callers handle that)

3. **`printMigrationMessage()`** - Shared helper for consistent user messages with context-specific "reason" parameter

**Updated call sites:**
- `manual_commit_git.go:SaveChanges()` - Uses reason "HEAD changed during session"
- `manual_commit_git.go:SaveTaskCheckpoint()` - Uses reason "HEAD changed during session"
- `manual_commit_hooks.go:InitializeSession()` - Uses reason "base commit changed after pull/rebase"

**Benefits:**
- Single source of truth for migration logic
- Consistent behavior across all code paths
- Easier to maintain and fix bugs
- Different messages per context while sharing core logic

## Summary of Unit Tests Added

Created `manual_commit_migration_test.go` with the following tests:

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_NoMigrationNeeded` | HEAD matches BaseCommit - no migration |
| `TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_MigrationWithBranchMove` | HEAD changed, shadow branch exists - renames branch |
| `TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_MigrationWithoutBranch` | HEAD changed, no shadow branch - just updates BaseCommit |
| `TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_NilState` | Nil state - returns empty result |
| `TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_EmptyBaseCommit` | Empty BaseCommit - returns empty result |
| `TestPrintMigrationMessage_NoMigration` | Verifies no panic on empty result |
| `TestMigrationResult_Fields` | Verifies struct field access |

The tests cover all code paths:
- No migration needed (HEAD matches)
- Migration with branch move (shadow branch exists)
- Migration without branch move (shadow branch was deleted by condensation)
- Edge cases (nil state, empty BaseCommit)

> DEVELOPER

can you review the tests about not testing core functionality or duplicating tests?

> AGENT

Looking at the tests I wrote:

**Should remove - trivial/no value:**

1. **`TestMigrationResult_Fields`** - Just tests that Go struct fields work. This is testing the language, not our code. Delete it.

2. **`TestPrintMigrationMessage_NoMigration`** - Only verifies "no panic" on an empty struct. Too trivial to be useful.

**Potentially redundant:**

3. **`TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_NilState`** and **`TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_EmptyBaseCommit`** - These test the same early return path (lines 41-43 in migration.go). Could be combined into one test with subtests, or just keep one.

**Core functionality tests (keep):**

4. **`TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_NoMigrationNeeded`** - Tests no-op when HEAD matches
5. **`TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_MigrationWithBranchMove`** - Tests the main migration path
6. **`TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_MigrationWithoutBranch`** - Tests the condensation scenario

Want me to remove the trivial tests and consolidate the edge cases?

   143→		t.Errorf("state.BaseCommit = %q, want %q", state.BaseCommit, secondCommit.String())
   144→	}
   145→
   146→	// Verify old shadow branch is gone
   147→	_, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(oldShadowBranch), true)
   148→	if err == nil {
   149→		t.Error("old shadow branch should have been deleted")
   150→	}
   151→
   152→	// Verify new shadow branch exists
   153→	newShadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(secondCommit.String())
   154→	_, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch), true)
   155→	if err != nil {
   156→		t.Errorf("new shadow branch should exist: %v", err)
   157→	}
   158→}

   182→	})

   185→	}

> DEVELOPER

Shadow branch migration can overwrite other sessions' checkpoints

Medium Severity

The migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded function creates the new shadow branch reference using SetReference without checking if a shadow branch at that name already exists from a different session. If Session A rebases to the same commit that Session B started from, Session A's migration will overwrite Session B's shadow branch reference, orphaning Session B's checkpoint commits. The orphaned commits aren't deleted but become unreferenced and eventually garbage collected.

> AGENT

Good catch. Let me trace through the scenario:

1. Session B starts at commit X → creates `entire/X` with checkpoints
2. Session A starts at commit Y → creates `entire/Y` with checkpoints  
3. Session A rebases Y → X (same as Session B's base)
4. Migration: `SetReference` overwrites `entire/X` with `entire/Y`'s commit hash
5. Session B's checkpoint commits become unreferenced → eventual GC

Let me fix this by checking if the target branch already exists.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01SfcrB7QexxstjC1rvbr2Fx
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go",
  "old_string": "\t// Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit\n\tresult.BranchMoved = true\n\tresult.OldBranch = oldShadowBranch\n\tresult.NewBranch = newShadowBranch\n\n\tnewRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)\n\n\t// Create new reference pointing to same commit as old shadow branch\n\tnewRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())\n\tif err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {\n\t\treturn result, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w\", newShadowBranch, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Delete old reference\n\tif err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {\n\t\t// Non-fatal: log but continue - the important thing is the new branch exists\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\\n\", oldShadowBranch, err)\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Old shadow branch exists - move it to new base commit\n\tnewRefName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(newShadowBranch)\n\n\t// Check if target shadow branch already exists (from another session)\n\t// If so, don't overwrite it - just delete our old branch and let new checkpoints\n\t// be added to the existing branch\n\tif _, err := repo.Reference(newRefName, true); err == nil {\n\t\t// Target branch exists - another session is using it\n\t\t// Delete our old branch (it would be orphaned anyway)\n\t\tif err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {\n\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\\n\", oldShadowBranch, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Don't set BranchMoved since we didn't actually move it\n\t\tstate.BaseCommit = currentHead\n\t\treturn result, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Target branch doesn't exist - safe to move\n\tresult.BranchMoved = true\n\tresult.OldBranch = oldShadowBranch\n\tresult.NewBranch = newShadowBranch\n\n\t// Create new reference pointing to same commit as old shadow branch\n\tnewRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(newRefName, oldRef.Hash())\n\tif err := repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {\n\t\treturn result, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create new shadow branch %s: %w\", newShadowBranch, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Delete old reference\n\tif err := repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName); err != nil {\n\t\t// Non-fatal: log but continue - the important thing is the new branch exists\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Warning: failed to remove old shadow branch %s: %v\\n\", oldShadowBranch, err)\n\t}"
}

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's the fix summary:

## Fix for Shadow Branch Overwrite Issue

**Problem:** When Session A rebases to the same commit that Session B started from, migration would overwrite Session B's shadow branch, orphaning its checkpoints.

**Solution:** Check if target shadow branch exists before moving:

```go
// Check if target shadow branch already exists (from another session)
if _, err := repo.Reference(newRefName, true); err == nil {
    // Target branch exists - another session is using it
    // Delete our old branch and let new checkpoints be added to existing branch
    repo.Storer.RemoveReference(oldRefName)
    state.BaseCommit = currentHead
    return result, nil  // BranchMoved = false
}
```

**Behavior after fix:**
- Session A's old shadow branch is deleted (would be orphaned anyway)
- Session A's `BaseCommit` is updated to the new HEAD
- Session B's shadow branch is preserved
- Session A's subsequent checkpoints will be added to the existing branch (alongside Session B's)

**Test added:** `TestMigrateShadowBranchIfNeeded_TargetBranchExists` verifies:
- Migration detects HEAD change (`Migrated = true`)
- Branch is NOT moved (`BranchMoved = false`)
- Session B's shadow branch is not overwritten
- Session A's old branch is cleaned up
